## Architecture & Repository Map

### Repository Layout
- `src/`: Python application frontend and control panel runtime.
  - `forgepact.py`: Single-file local web/desktop application (`http://127.0.0.1:8780`; `8766` before 1.4.6, see "Panel port" below). Manages port selection, configuration persistence (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Hero_Siege\forgepact.json`), background game process detection / auto-apply watcher, PE binary patching / backup / restoration via `AuriePatcher.exe`, and file-based IPC dispatch to `bp_ipc/cmd.txt`. Since the UI port (2026-09-24, [ADR 0004](../../adr/0004-forgepact-panel-frontend-is-svelte-and-vite-on-a-python-backend.md)) it holds no page: the old `HTML` string and `POLL_POLICY_JS` are gone, and `do_GET` serves the built `panel/` from `PANEL_DIST` (`sys._MEIPASS/panel` when frozen, `panel/dist` from source, overridden by the `FORGEPACT_PANEL_DIST` environment variable). `/` answers `index.html` with `Cache-Control: no-cache`; any other non-`/api` path answers a file only when its resolved path is inside `PANEL_DIST` (404 JSON otherwise), typed from a small suffix table; a missing `index.html` answers `/` with a 503 JSON naming `npm --prefix panel run build`. The `/api` routes and every command they send are unchanged. `POLL_WATCHED_FIELDS` stays here as the Python half of the poll-policy parity test.
  - `panel_icons.py`: **Removed in the UI port.** The icon sprite and `PANEL_ICON_MAP` it held moved to `panel/src/icons.js`.
  - `offline_launcher.py`: The HS Offline Launcher engine embedded in ForgePact (MIT; `UPSTREAM_REVISION` pins the HS-Offline-Launcher commit its audited helpers came from, and `UPSTREAM_DEFINITIONS` lists them). It owns the Win32 process snapshot (`processes`), the EAC service query (`eac_service_status`), PE validation (`validate_game`, `_exe_facts`), the Steam runtime lookup, the safety gate (`launch_safety_blocker`) and the single launch lock. The standalone UI, config discovery and polling loop are deliberately absent — the caller supplies the executable path. **Importing this module starts nothing**, which `tests/test_offline_launcher.py::test_source_import_needs_no_separate_launcher_and_starts_nothing` pins.

    The superproject's `tools/hs_drive_mcp/launcher_bridge.py` **imports this file by path** — it never copies or modifies it — and the superproject's `tests/test_hs_drive_mcp_engine_bridge.py` pins the names it calls as `ENGINE_SYMBOLS`, asserts the import still spawns no process and starts no thread, and asserts a checkout without this file refuses by name rather than raising. So a rename here fails that suite rather than surfacing at a tool call: when changing a public name in `offline_launcher.py`, update `ENGINE_SYMBOLS` in the same change. `docs/tools/hs-drive-mcp.md` describes the server.
- `panel/`: The control panel's frontend, Svelte 5 + Vite (the hub's versions), built to static files that `src/forgepact.py` serves and PyInstaller bundles ([ADR 0004](../../adr/0004-forgepact-panel-frontend-is-svelte-and-vite-on-a-python-backend.md)). Private, version `0.0.0` (ForgePact's version stays in `__version__`), devDependencies only; `engines` is Node `^20.19.0 || >=22.12.0`. Files under it are LF (`panel/.gitattributes`), except `*.woff2` and `*.png`, which the same file marks binary so a CRLF byte pair inside a font or screenshot is never rewritten.
  - `src/`: `main.js` mounts `App.svelte` (the shell, sidebar and tab switching); `tabs/{Setup,Modifiers,World,Loot,Mods}.svelte` hold each tab's markup; `app.css` is the redesigned panel's whole look, drawn only from the custom properties in `tokens.css` (generated from `panel/design/figma-export.json`, one `:root[data-theme]` block per palette), and since forgepact-ui-ship also its motion, the export's `motion.notes` built with CSS transitions and `@starting-style` on the motion tokens alone: hover colours eased in on the `:hover` rules only (a palette swap or a poll never runs a colour transition), a `.97` press on buttons, both tooltips and the Enabled mods tray scaling in from their trigger, the toasts rising in and sinking out, and a turned-off entry's placeholder copy fading out; `[data-instant]` and `:focus-visible` keep every keyboard action unanimated, and the `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` rule removes every movement and scale (transform, translate, scale, rotate) while the opacity fades and the hover colour fades stay (the owner's F4, and "Keep colour fades too", recorded in the export as `amendments.ship`); `fonts.css` declares one `@font-face` each for `IBM Plex Sans` and `IBM Plex Mono` (the variable weight range `100 700`, normal style, `font-display: block`, a relative `url(./fonts/...)` in `format('woff2')`, nothing from the network) and is imported by `main.js` directly before `app.css`, and `tokens.css` names them as the body and mono families (the file's own comment, "no rule uses these families yet", predates the restyle, and `fonts.css` is frozen); `panel/index.html` preloads both files (`<link rel="preload" as="font" type="font/woff2" crossorigin>`, owner 2026-09-26; `crossorigin` because fonts are fetched in CORS mode, and Vite rewrites each link to the same hashed `/assets/*.woff2` the built CSS names, adding no asset); the faces are `src/fonts/IBMPlexSans/IBMPlexSans-Variable.woff2` and `src/fonts/IBMPlexMono/IBMPlexMono-Variable.woff2`, byte-identical to IBM/plex's upright variable `Var-Roman` files at tags `@ibm/plex-sans-variable@0.2.0` and `@ibm/plex-mono-variable@1.0.0`, each beside its `LICENSE.txt` (SIL Open Font License 1.1, credited in `CREDITS.md`); `panel.js` is what the old page's inline script did once its markup was on screen (read `/api/state` in `boot()`, draw the data-driven rows through `row()`, wire every control, keep the status chips current, poll), reached from `main.js` through `start()` once `App.svelte` is mounted; `refreshSavedControls()` repaints the saved controls after every drained write; `api.js` is the write queue and the save indicator; `state.svelte.js` holds the `/api/state` payload as `ST`; `poll-policy.js`, `nav.js`, `mods-columns.js` and `mods-sync.js` are the poll policy, tab/sub-tab navigation, the Mods column balancer and the parent/child sync helpers as plain JS modules; `enabled-mods.js` is `enabledControls(cfg)`, the pure rule for which mods are on (the boolean mods when true, the skill timer when not `off`, density when `density_on` and above 1, every switched slider whose switch is on and whose value is off its default; never `auto_apply`, `enemy_speed_ct`, the child options, `game_exe`, `theme` or the Satanic pools), returning the id of the control that turns each one off; `lib/enabled-mods-list.js` (`renderEnabledMods(cfg)`, `quickDisable(control)`) is the `Enabled mods` list, a plain-JS renderer filling the `#enabledMods` shell that `App.svelte` places directly after `.control-dock`; it renders `enabledControls(ST.cfg)` in document order, takes each entry's name from its row's `.lbl` and value from its `.val` (one exception, the owner's call: the density entry, whose switch borrows the multiplier's row, is named by its card's `h2`, `Monster Density`, while the World row keeps its label and aria-labels `Density multiplier`), and whose `Turn off` sets that control off and dispatches `change` on it, so the control's own handler posts (no `/api` route in the list's files); each list it builds carries `data-instant`, so a list rebuilt while the tray is open appears at once; `lib/enabled-mods-form.js` decides the list's two forms, the inline row while its entries fit on one line and the popover tray under the count in the rail once they overfill it (`nextForm`, `HYSTERESIS_PX` 160, the switch held while a hand is on the page and for `HOLD_IDLE_MS` 400 after), and owns the tray's open and close (Escape, an outside click or a tab change close it; only the pointer's own open or close lifts `data-instant` for the motion); `lib/enabled-mods-undo.js` is Turn off's safety net: the undo toast (`UNDO_VISIBLE_MS` 8000, paused while hovered, focused or hidden; a hidden toast's timer never starts again, since the pointer leaving a used toast once restarted it and hid the next Turn off's toast early; Undo re-dispatches `change` on the control), its polite live-region text, the focus hand-off after a Turn off, and the reflow freeze whose placeholder keeps the removed entry's place under the pointer for up to `FREEZE_MAX_MS` 2500 (the commit timeout), the first placeholder after a pointer's Turn off being an inert, aria-hidden copy of the entry that fades out; `lib/remembered-value.js` draws a switched-off slider's value faintly beside its `off` value box (`.val-remembered`, presentational only); `lib/review-fixes.js` holds the restyle review's code-side fixes that react to the unchanged handlers' DOM: the idle switch (`data-live` on a slider switch only while its mod is listed) and findings 9, 13 and 15, with 18, 19 and 24 in `app.css`; `lib/enabled-mods-copy.js` is the only place the list's approved copy lives (`UNDO_TEXTS` and the entry title's joiner, from the export's `amendments`); `theme.js` is `THEMES` and `applyTheme(name)`, which sets `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` (the `#theme` select sits in the Setup tab's last card, `Appearance`, since the owner's polish pass of 2026-09-25; it was in `#statusbar` before); `lib/theme-picker.js` (forgepact-ui-ship, finish review F2, the owner's "Build the ThemePicker") draws that select as the design's ThemePicker: an id-less `.theme-picker-trigger` button (named by the row's "Theme" and its value) and a `.theme-picker-list` listbox whose options show three swatches read from `tokens.css`'s own rules at run time, with the full listbox keyboard; the select stays underneath, clipped to 1px, `aria-hidden` and out of the tab order, and choosing sets its value and dispatches `change` only on a real change, so its handler, the oracle and the posts are unchanged; `lib/plugin-warning.js` opens, closes and places the plugin warning's two tooltips (the icon button in `#pluginWarning` beside Apply all, and its twin beside `#saveIndicator`, kept in step with `#pluginWarningText` without editing `status()`); `lib/slider-note.js` shows an idle slider row's `.note[data-note]` as a tooltip above its row by setting `data-tooltip` on it (hover after `OPEN_DELAY_MS` 300, at once within `INSTANT_MS` 400 of another note closing or on keyboard focus; closed by pointer leave, blur, Escape or a change in the row; a slider at its default has no note since the owner's decision of 2026-09-26, so the tooltip opens only for an idle row holding a value moved and not yet saved) while `app.css` draws `[data-tooltip]` out of the flow with `pointer-events: none`, so no row moves: a note shown in the flow moved the rows below under the pointer as it hid, and the behaviour oracle's replay missed 90 clicks; `icons.js` is the icon sprite and `PANEL_ICON_MAP` (formerly `src/panel_icons.py`). Rows the page renders from `ST` (spawners, keys, drops, stats, percent stats, Satanic pools) stay data-driven; every slider row but Monster Density carries a `.slider-switch` checkbox `#sw_<id>` (`.` becomes `_`, e.g. `#sw_stats_exp`) with `data-switch="<id>"`, from `row()` for the table rows and from `World.svelte`/`Loot.svelte` for the four top-level ranges. `#den_on` stays density's switch. Each note `row()` writes (the keys, stats and percent_stats rows, and the Loot `mining_ore` note, which `row()` now writes too) has the id `note-<section>-<key>`, and its range names it in `aria-describedby`, so a screen reader reads the note with the slider (the owner's F2; a hidden note still gives its text, an empty one gives none).
  - `tests/`: browser tooling on `playwright-core`, driving the installed Microsoft Edge (`channel: 'msedge'`, headless; no browser is downloaded). `lib/browser.mjs` launches it and starts/stops `../tests/panel_sandbox_server.py` (it waits up to `SANDBOX_START_TIMEOUT_MS`, 120 s, for a sandbox's port (`startTimeoutMs` overrides it; `startDelayMs` passes the server's `--start-delay`, a start that misses a shorter limit on any machine), and kills one that misses it before dropping its seed file, and a port line that arrives after that no longer counts as a start; every line a sandbox prints on stderr reaches the suite's stderr prefixed `[sandbox :<port>]`, or `[sandbox pid <n>]` before it has a port; a failed start or a failed `sandbox.state()` carries `describe()`: whether the sandbox is still running or exited, with its code, and its last stderr lines; see "Parallel load and the panel sandbox" under "The build half"); `oracle.mjs` records and replays the behaviour oracle (a replay mismatch also lists `requests`, how each of that step's POSTs ended: the status it was answered with, Chromium's `net::` error it failed with, or no answer, and a save that never ends names its step, those POSTs and the sandbox's state); `behaviour-oracle.json` is the recording, taken from the legacy page (`recordedFrom: "legacy"`): every control's POST bodies and `cmd.txt` lines per action, and it is never edited or re-recorded; `oracle-derive.mjs` (`npm run oracle:derive`) generates `behaviour-oracle-derived.json` from the legacy file's `controls` list and the switch, enabled-mods and theme contract, deterministically, for the controls the legacy page never had (`#sw_*`, `#enabledMods .quick-disable[data-for=…]`, `#theme`); the derived file holds no recorded values, only steps whose expected posts and `cmd.txt` lines are either another step of the same replay (`same: <step>`, e.g. a switch turned off must send what the slider's own `min` step sent) or a literal the contract fixes (`is: […]`), and `oracle.mjs replay --oracle <legacy> --derived <derived> --supplement <supplement>` (`npm run oracle:replay`) runs all three on fresh sandboxes and checks coverage against the union of their `controls`; `behaviour-oracle-gems.json` is the supplement, a second recording from a legacy page (`recordedFrom: "legacy"`, `sourceRev` the ForgePact commit it was recorded from, `f1e2f57`, origin/main's last pre-port page with the Gems of Incarnation controls): a fixed scenario over `#mod_gem_mythic`, `#mod_gem_maxroll` and the mod filter (`#gemfilter_toggle` and the list's buttons and boxes, scoped under `#gemfilter_panel`), recorded by `oracle.mjs record --legacy --only gems --src <dir> --source-rev <sha> --out tests/behaviour-oracle-gems.json` from `git archive <sha> src` extracted outside the checkout; that recorder refuses when the page's controls beyond the legacy file's are not exactly those three, never writes `behaviour-oracle.json`, and like it the supplement is never edited, only re-recorded from the same `sourceRev`; `oracle-derive.mjs --supplement <supplement>` also derives the gem switches' on/off/on/Turn off steps from it (the derived file names it as `supplementFrom`); `lib/oracle-relocate.mjs` maps the supplement's two navigation steps (`tab:mods`, `subtab:qol`) to `tab:loot`, since the gem controls moved to the Loot tab: `SUPPLEMENT_RELOCATION` is the whole map, and `relocate()` returns a copy that changes only a moved step's `control`, refusing a step that recorded any post or command, a key or value that is not `tab:…`/`subtab:…`, and a key no step uses; it is applied in exactly two places, `replaySupplement` in `oracle.mjs` and the supplement branch of `oracle-derive.mjs`'s `derive()`, never to the legacy recording or the recorder, and `oracle-relocate.test.js` pins that contract; `behaviour-oracle-primeevil.json` is the key supplement, a third recording from a legacy page (`recordedFrom: "legacy"`, `sourceRev` `841c2db`, origin/main at 1.4.7 with the Prime Evil Parts slider): the Loot tab, then that slider's max, min, `+`, `-` and typed midpoint, then Apply all, recorded by `oracle.mjs record --legacy --only primeevil --src <dir> --source-rev <sha> --out tests/behaviour-oracle-primeevil.json`, which refuses unless the page's controls beyond the legacy and Gems files are exactly that slider and never writes either of them; `oracle.mjs replay --key-supplement <file>` replays it on its own fresh sandbox exactly as recorded, and `oracle-derive.mjs --key-supplement <file>` derives the slider's switch steps from it (appended after the theme steps; the derived file names it as `keySupplementFrom`); `insertAddedKeys()`, also in `lib/oracle-relocate.mjs`, is the one transform for a key the backend added after a recording was made: in every step whose `cmd.txt` lines are a full key reset (they hold `dungeonkey del 12`) it inserts `droprate group primeevil 1` right after the `droprate group colosfrag` line (`ADDED_KEYS`), changes nothing else, never mutates its input, and throws on a full reset without exactly one anchor or already carrying the line (so the key supplement itself is refused); replay applies it to the legacy recording and the Gems supplement only (70 legacy steps gain the line; the Gems file has no full reset), never to the key supplement or in the recorder, and `oracle-relocate.test.js` pins it too; `oracle-derive.test.js` asserts the committed derived file is byte-identical to a regeneration; `polish.e2e.mjs` (`npm run e2e:polish [-- --shots <dir>]`) checks the owner's polish pass (see the Command Reference row); `perf.e2e.mjs` (`npm run e2e:perf`) measures how fast the panel answers, with rendering on, and holds every interaction to the owner's hard budgets (see "Panel responsiveness (2026-09-25)"), on `lib/perf-page.mjs` (the in-page samplers: rAF frame deltas, Event Timing, result paint, mutation counts, and `performance.mark` windows a trace is cut to) and `lib/perf-stats.mjs` (median, nearest-rank p95, frame counts, trace attribution, the target and hard budgets, `panelSrcDigest`), unit-tested by `perf-budget.test.js` over `fixtures/perf/trace-mini.json`; `perf/perf-baseline.json` and `perf/perf-after.json` are that workorder's raw results (before and after its fixes, each with the `panelSrcDigest` of the source it measured), tracked and never rewritten by a run; `gems.e2e.mjs` (`npm run e2e:gems [-- --shots <dir>]`) checks the DOM facts the oracle cannot see: `#gemsCard` as the Loot tab's last card and none of the gem controls on Mods, both switches off at the defaults, their Enabled mods entries and Turn off, the mod filter's summary, list, refusal and save, the list's Satanic pool classes and strings, its search and All mods / Enabled / Disabled filter (which show and hide rows, never untick one), the unsaved-changes cue, and the card following the Loot tab's search and Modified filter; `ui.e2e.mjs` is the e2e suite, carrying every `passed.push('<label>')` check of the four deleted `tests/*_browser.js` harnesses under the same labels; `enabled-mods-form.e2e.mjs` (`npm run e2e:form`) proves the Enabled mods list's two forms in the browser (inline while the entries fit, the tray once they overfill, back only past the hysteresis band), the entries' names and the remembered value on a switched-off slider, read only from the DOM and the sandbox's saved state, and `enabled-mods-form.test.js` proves the form rule itself (`nextForm`, `holdActive`) under plain node; `review-fixes.e2e.mjs` (`npm run e2e:review`) proves the restyle review's fixes on the page (the tray popover's open, focus and close paths, the undo toast and that Undo posts exactly what turning the control on posts, that a used toast's timer leaves the next toast its full time (`undo-toast-own-timer`), the focus hand-off, the reflow freeze under the pointer, the held form switch, the idle switch, and findings 9, 13, 15, 18, 19 and 24), and `review-fixes.test.js` proves their pure decisions (where focus goes after a Turn off, when the form switch is held, the primary Setup button, an entry's title); `finish.e2e.mjs` (`npm run e2e:finish`, forgepact-ui-ship round 2) proves the Impeccable finish review's eight owner-approved fixes: the redrawn Setup and Mods rail icons (F1); the theme picker's ARIA, keyboard, sync with the select and `data-theme`, hidden select and swatches, and its posts held to exactly the derived oracle's three theme steps, by pointer and by keyboard, with nothing on open, close, Escape or the same palette (F2); the drawn check on the settled save indicator and a valid Satanic selection (F3, F6); the rarity note hidden at rest (F4); the chain warning's mono run with idle polls writing nothing (F5); no ✓, ↳ or ↺ glyph on any tab (F6); 32/32 and 20/20 gutters (F7); the launcher description's size and colour and the helmet's line break (F8); and, from the PR #100 review, Restore defaults' drawn `restore` icon in place of its ↺ (`finish-restore-icon`); `theme-picker.test.js` proves the swatch reading and the listbox's moves; `motion.e2e.mjs` (`npm run e2e:motion`, forgepact-ui-ship) proves the motion: a recorder logs every `transitionrun` with its CSSTransition's duration and easing, compared with the motion tokens' computed values, and each positive has its control (a hover's colour transition against a palette swap that runs none, the pointer's press, tray and Turn off against the keyboard's, a list rebuilt in the open tray, a resize after the close); under `page.emulateMedia({ reducedMotion: 'reduce' })`, asserted off before and on after, both tooltips fade without scaling and no transform, translate, scale or rotate transition runs anywhere, while at least two opacity fades and one hover colour fade still do (`reduced-motion-no-movement`, each count its own positive control); it also proves each note-carrying range's `aria-describedby` on every tab and that the plugin warning's tooltip enters with no `data-starting`; `screens.mjs` captures every tab (`setup`, `modifiers`, `world`, `loot`, `mods-qol`, `mods-items`) at 1280×800 and 900×700 and compares two runs with `pixelmatch`; `*.test.js` are `node --test` units for the poll policy, navigation, column balancer, `enabledControls` (`enabled-mods.test.js`: empty on defaults, one case per rule and exclusion), `theme.js` (`theme.test.js`), the derivation (`oracle-derive.test.js`) and the supplement's navigation relocation (`oracle-relocate.test.js`), run by `scripts/test.mjs`; `tokens.test.js` (a fixture export's round trip through `tokens-from-export.mjs`, a negative control that must report its planted differences, and the comparison of `src/tokens.css` with the real `design/figma-export.json`, the one test allowed to skip, and only while that export is absent) and `contrast.test.js` (the contrast maths: `#000000` on `#ffffff` is 21, `#777777` on `#ffffff` is 4.48 and fails 4.5, and an alpha-composited case) run in the same `npm test`. The restyle tooling, proven here before any real export exists: `design-match.mjs --export <f> --figma-dir <d> --out <d> [--checks texts,tokens,flatness,composites,placement,runtime]` loads the built panel in the sandbox and, per export screen, checks that the screen's texts are on the page, that each `selectorTokens` entry's computed style equals its variable in every palette (a non-default palette through `data-theme`, no POST; a selector that exists only in some state, such as `.enabled-mod` or `.value-stepper>.val.off`, is checked in the first state the page's own controls reach, and then in each screen's `state`, a sandbox started through `--offline` and `--seed`; a bare selector the export also lists a variant of is measured on an element in none of its variants), counts flatness violations against the export's `borders` list, and writes `<out>/compare/<tab>-<width>.png`, the Figma image beside the render; a check left out of `--checks` prints `<name>: skipped`, and any failure exits 1. Its structure-only mode, `--structure` (`--checks texts,placement,runtime`, no `--figma-dir`), compares the export's shape with the current build and no pixel or token: the texts, each `selectorTokens` entry that names a `component` placed inside that component's `selector` on every screen, and no version, path or port baked into a text (`lib/design-structure.mjs`, unit-tested without a browser by `design-structure.test.js`, and end to end by `structure-selftest.mjs` on `fixtures/design/export-structure.json` and `export-structure-wrong.json`). `flatness-selftest.mjs` runs that flatness rule on static pages with a known answer each (a flat page, an unlisted border, the same border allowlisted, a card in a card, a bordered element under a bordered ancestor, bordered form controls) and prints `flatness-selftest: <n> cases, <k> wrong` last. `tokens-selftest.mjs` does the same for the variant rule (class, attribute and child-combinator variants, a descendant that is not one) and prints `tokens-selftest: <n> cases, <k> wrong` last. `lib/design-*.mjs` hold the pieces they share. `fonts-load.mjs` asks the built panel's `document.fonts.load()` for both bundled families, with a family nobody declares as the control that must return no face (a declared but unused `@font-face` is never downloaded, so only an explicit load proves the files decode), and prints `fonts-load: 2 loaded, control 0` last. `fixtures/design/` is tracked and independent of any look: `export-mini.json` (palettes `alpha` default, `beta`, `gamma`; one variable per token unit rule; every contrast pair passes), `export-wrong.json` (planted failures: a missing text, two mismatched tokens per palette, an empty `borders` list, a failing contrast pair), the three state fixtures `export-state.json` (two screens seeded game offline with density x3 and map reveal on, whose texts only that state shows), `export-state-none.json` (the same screens without `state`, so those texts are missing) and `export-state-badkey.json` (an unknown config key the sandbox refuses), and `screens/world-1280.png`, `screens/mods-items-900.png`, plain generated images standing in for Figma frames. No tool, test or fixture names a real palette.
  - `scripts/`: `test.mjs` is `npm test` (every `tests/*.test.js` under `node --test`); `export-icons.mjs` exports the icon pack. `tokens-from-export.mjs <export.json> [--out <file>]` writes `tokens.css` from a Figma export (to stdout unless `--out`): the palette marked `default: true` in `:root`, and each other palette's COLOR variables in a `:root[data-theme="<name>"]` block; it also exports the generator and the parser/comparator `tokens.test.js` imports. `contrast-check.mjs <export.json>` computes the WCAG 2.x contrast ratio of every `contrastPairs` entry in every palette (a palette's colours over the default's; an fg with alpha composited over the pair's bg, a bg with alpha over that palette's `color/bg/base`), compares the unrounded ratio with the pair's `minRatio`, prints a `PASS` or `FAIL` line per pair and palette and `contrast: <pairs> pairs x <palettes> palettes, <k> failing` last, and exits 1 when any pair fails or the file does not parse. None of these has an `npm` script; they run with `node`.
  - `vite.config.js`: dev server on port 5178 (`strictPort`; the hub uses 5177, HS-Offline-Tracker 5176) proxying `/api` to `http://127.0.0.1:8780`; build output `dist/`.
  - `dist/`: the build output. Gitignored; `build_release.py` refuses to package without `dist/index.html`.
- `assets/panel/`: the README's panel screenshots (forgepact-ui-ship, 2026-09-26): `modifiers-1280.png`, `world-1280.png`, `loot-1280.png`, `mods-qol-1280.png` and `mods-items-1280.png`, copied from `npm --prefix ForgePact/panel run screens` at 1280 wide. They come from the test sandbox, so each shows the chip "Game open · plugin missing" and the plugin warning icon; Setup is left out because the sandbox writes its temporary `Hero_Siege.exe` path, which carries the machine's user name, onto that page (owner, 2026-09-26: accepted). Re-render them after a visual change, from a tree whose version is the one being released (the status bar shows it).
- `panel/DESIGN.md` and `panel/.impeccable/design.json` (forgepact-ui-ship, 2026-09-26): the panel's design system as built, in the Impeccable format: `DESIGN.md` carries the tokens in its frontmatter (colours, type roles, radii, spacing, component variants) and the narrative (the Graphite Console, colours, typography, layout, depth, shapes, components including the theme picker and the drawn check marks, motion, do's and don'ts); the sidecar is its machine-readable half (each colour in all three palettes, motion tokens, `ds-` component snippets on `var(--*)` tokens), which the Impeccable live panel renders. Both are committed and hold no per-machine state (the Impeccable hook's own files live at the hub root, which the hub `.gitignore` covers). The Figma export stays the contract (`panel/design/figma-export.json`); update both files when a change alters what the panel draws.
- `plugin/`: Native GameMaker mod plugin implementation.
  - `ModuleMain.cpp`: C++20 dynamic library source for `BloodPactPlugin`. Implements Aurie module lifecycle (`ModuleInitialize`), hooks GameMaker engine routines via YYToolkit, manages frame event callbacks (`EVENT_FRAME`), polls commands from `bp_ipc/cmd.txt`, logs responses to `bp_ipc/out.txt`, applies throttled density/spawn overrides, manipulates drop tables and LoadDrops gates, projects HUD head labels, and exports live item statistics to `bp_ipc/itemstats.json`.
  - `BUILD.md`: Build requirements, compilation instructions, and differences between shipping (`/DFORGEPACT_RELEASE`) and research builds.
  - `include/ForgePact/AutoProspectMod.hpp`: The auto-prospect decision core (issue #9, Stage B) - when an insert into the ProspectGrid counts, when to invoke, when to refuse and what to say, plus the recorded shape's names (`activationFunc`, `activationArgs`, `"ProspectGrid"`). Stage C adds the move pass: when to ask for it (`MoveMaterials`, once per landed insert, just before the invoke), which cells (those the adapter flagged as materials - the core has no item-type value), and what each cell's report means (`ClassifyMove`). Game-independent (names no runtime interface), so `tests/auto_prospect_harness.cpp` compiles it whole; `ModuleMain.cpp`'s `Hook_AutoProspectInsert`/`AutoProspectTick`/`ApMovePass` are the only code that touches the game for it.
  - `include/ForgePact/CraftMatsMod.hpp`: The crafting-materials decision core (issue #14; the player build's `craftmats`, ForgePact 1.4.5, off by default). Game-independent - standard headers only, no runtime interface - so `tests/craft_mats_harness.cpp` compiles it whole. It decides everything the adapter in `ModuleMain.cpp` (`Cm*`, after `HandleProspectCommand`) acts on: the count (`CountAnswer`: the game's own `CountInventoryItem` answer, plus the two special tabs' stash count only with the switch on and inside the crafting route; an unreadable stash leaves the game's count and is named once); the walk (`MustWalk`/`KeepWalk`: display frames reuse one walk per game frame, the press walks fresh); the needs (`BeginFind`/`OnDecode`/`OnFindCount`/`Needs`: each count inside `CraftFindRecipeItems` takes the amount of the latest `PilipaliDecrypt` before it, the last count of a run after one decode is the one used, and a count with no decode makes the needs unreadable); the press gate (`PressStep`: the game's own press when no stash count was added, otherwise a refusal under its one named, counted reason - `already-served` when the record already served a press, `unpaired` when it cannot be paired, `unnumbered-row` when the adapter could not number the recipe row, `other-row` when it belongs to another row; until issue #80 all four were one `unreadable`); per material a craft needs, need N, bag count k, stash count s -> take min(N-k, s) (`Plan`, from its Phase 0 arithmetic); the split (`Split`: whole entries first, then a partial from the last); the move outcome (`OnMoveReport`: confirmed only when the source dropped and the destination rose by exactly the amount, not-taken when neither side changed, anything else a loss that turns the mod off for the session; since issue #80 a take whose count edit's `ItemCheckHash` call did not dispatch - the adapter feeds `OnHashFailed` with the item's key before the report - is never confirmed: re-read as put back it is not-taken under `hash-failed`, re-read as moved it is a loss); the craft gate (`MayCraft`: only when every take is confirmed); the consume check (`OnConsume`); the save (`SaveDue`: only after a confirmed move); and the per-press, refusal and loss lines. Its source enum is `StashMaterialTab = 1, StashSocketTab = 2` and nothing else (`docs/crafting-materials-research.md`, `## Ship design`). Phase 1e adds `CraftMatsKeptMap`, the kept stash map's currency rule - current only when the game's own `GetItemMap(9)` return refreshed it after the latest character load or room change, never on `ds_exists` alone (map indices are reused) - pinned by the harness's `kept_map` baseline and target scenarios and fed, so far, only by the research build's `mapkeep`.
  - `include/ForgePact/StashMoveAllMod.hpp`: The "Move all into the stash" decision core (ForgePact #68; the player build's `stashmoveall`/`stashmove` and F4, 2.1.0, off by default). Game-independent - standard headers only - so `tests/stash_move_all_harness.cpp` compiles it whole. It decides everything the adapter in `ModuleMain.cpp` (`Sma*`, the `stashmoveall, stashmove` block after the stash and bag verbs, and the `stashmoveall button` block after it) acts on: the switch (off by default, off for the session after a loss); the hotkey edge (`KeyEdge`: one run per press, only on, in the foreground, with the stash listed and no Alt, Ctrl or Shift held); the in-game button's side (`ButtonStep`: the node exists only while on with the stash and the bag's Sort button listed and Sort visible; `PressInNode`: a left press whose GUI point lies inside the node's bbox is a press, the poll route Live 1g measured; `NoteButtonPress`/`TakeButtonPress`: a press taken once by the frame tick under the key's guard; `ButtonRefused`: a node the adapter could not make is reported once, not tried again until the stash reopens or the switch is turned on again, and never turns the mod off); the plan (`Plan`/`PlanWith`: the bag view on show, each item once, row-major by its top-left cell, its footprint from its cells; the sources and destinations; the route per item under `kMeasuredRoutes`, the routes `docs/stash-move-research.md` § Decision recorded - `newMaterial`, `wholeStackMerge` and `socketMerge` on, `socketNew` and `socketWholeStackMerge` off, so the Socketable tab takes a single socketable whose identity has a node there, from the bag's Socket view only, and a stack of more than one or a new kind stays in the bag - Live 1f measured that tab's merge with one unit only, and `wholeStackMerge` was measured on the Materials tab); the route again at the point of use (`RouteAtUse`: a stackable's route from its identity's sum re-read on the shown tab just before its call, whatever the plan said - above 0 a merge of the whole count, 0 a placement, unread a skip; the round-2 review found two bag items of one identity duplicating a unit when the plan's route stood); the room check (`Room`, `MayCall`: a free block of the footprint, or a stack of the identity, on the shown tab's own cells, else a skip with nothing called); the outcome (`Decide`: moved only with the tab on show unchanged, the bag cell empty and the key at the answer's cell or the stack risen by the count; skipped on a game refusal with both sides unchanged; an owner step that was not dispatched, or after which the key still answers on map 0, unconfirmed; unconfirmed otherwise); `Record` (a skip continues, a loss stops and turns the mod off, keeping the reason); and every line, the state line reading `state=off-for-this-session <the button's fields> reason=<reason>` after a loss (the fields kept, the free-text reason last).
  - `include/ForgePact/RestartAnytimeMod.hpp`: The "Restart zone at any time" decision core (issue #8, `restartanytime`) - the site script (`UiSetFocus`), the identifying member and value (`uiNodeCallstack` = `PauseRestart`), the gate member and its ready value (`manualDisable` = false), `RestartAnytimeModel::Decide` (Pass or Write) and the armed/pending/blind flags and four counters. Game-independent: no `RValue`, no builtin call.
  - `include/ForgePact/IncarnationGemsMod.hpp`: The Gems of Incarnation decision core (ForgePact 1.4.6; `gemmythic`, `gemfilter`, `gemmaxroll`). Game-independent - standard headers only, no runtime interface - so `tests/incarnation_gems_harness.cpp` compiles it whole. It holds the gem's identity (item type 15, base 136, `c` 0), the per-build tables (512 Mythic seeds per drop `n`, each with its affix stats, and the tier-4 range per affix stat; schema 2 JSON), the drop decision (`DropSeed`: a Mythic seed for the drop's own `n`, among the seeds carrying the most wanted mods when a filter is set), the filter's parser (`ParseFilter`) and the dress (`Dress`/`MaxRoll`). It avoids `std::min`/`std::max`: `ModuleMain.cpp` includes `<windows.h>` without `NOMINMAX`, so those break the plugin build while the harness still compiles.
  - `include/ForgePact/PetLootUnstickMod.hpp`: The "Pet moves on from loot it cannot pick up" decision core (issue #94, `petunstick`, off by default; `docs/pet-loot-stuck-research.md`). `PetLootStuckWatch` and `PetLootRepickRing` are game-independent - frame numbers, target ids and a distance in pixels, never an instance - so `tests/pet_loot_unstick_harness.cpp` compiles them whole. The watch answers "give this target up" on the frame the same target's run within `kPetLootStuckRadiusPx` (160) reaches `kPetLootStuckFrames` (90) consecutive frames, and re-arms on answering, so a target taken straight back and still in reach is given up again every 90 frames. `PetLootRepickRing` (with its kind enum `PetLootKind { Other, Ground, Coin }` and `PetLootRepick`) keeps the last eight give-ups and decides whether a live target is one of them taken back within `kPetLootHoldFrames`, after the pet left it or straight back on the next tick, counting it once per give-up by kind. `kPetLootHoldFrames` (600) is also the value `PetLootUnstickTick()` in `ModuleMain.cpp` writes into the given-up ground item's `itemCompanionTimer`, and only after `variable_instance_exists` says the item carries that name (otherwise it writes nothing to the item and counts `timer absent=`). `PetLootUnstickMod` holds the switch, the watch, the ring and the counters `petunstick 0` prints (`StatLine()` / `FullStatLine()`: held back, coins released, longest same-target run between give-ups, where the ticks went, and the read-refusals); the tick's own counters (`give-up failed=`, `other kind=`, `timer absent=`) live in `ModuleMain.cpp`, and `PetLootLocalStatSuffix()` appends them with the ring's `re-picked while held=` and its ground/coin split.
  - `include/ForgePact/ExitSafeThread.hpp`: `ForgePact::ExitSafeThread`, how a module global owns a background thread. The `std::thread` is heap-held and freed only after `JoinFor` has joined it, and the holder has no destructor, so the game's `ExitProcess` never destroys a joinable thread (Known Limitations item 26). It owns the coop receive thread. Game-independent: `tests/coop_thread_exit_probe.cpp` compiles it whole into a probe DLL.
  - `include/ForgePact/FrameProfiler.hpp`: `ForgePact::FrameProfiler`, the `frameprof` sampling profiler (2026-09-28, see "Frame profiler (`frameprof`)" below). A background thread (an `ExitSafeThread` held by a never-freed singleton) pauses the frame thread `rate` times a second in `CaptureOnce` - `SuspendThread`, `GetThreadContext`, a guarded `memcpy` of the live stack into a buffer allocated beforehand, `ResumeThread`, and nothing else - then, after the resume, `WalkCopy` moves every pointer into the old stack range into the copy and unwinds it with `RtlVirtualUnwind`, finding unwind entries by binary search in each module's own `.pdata` (`ModuleMap`, never `RtlLookupFunctionEntry`). `Symbolizer` names frames from the game's compiled-code table (`GmlEntry` = `YYGMLFuncs`, walked both ways from one row), the built-ins the adapter resolved and each system DLL's exports; `Classify` buckets a stack from the innermost frame out. `Profiler::BuildReport` writes `bp_ipc\perf\<stem>.json`, `.stacks.txt` and `.txt`. Game-independent (Win32 and the standard library, no `RValue`), so `tests/frame_profiler_harness.cpp` compiles it whole. Its companion `FrameProfilerBuiltins.hpp` holds `kBuiltinNames`, the 429 built-in names the adapter resolves. Avoids `std::min`/`std::max` for the `NOMINMAX` reason above.
  - `include/ForgePact/FarSleep.hpp`: `ForgePact::FarSleep`, far scenery sleep (2026-09-28, `farsleep`, see "Far scenery sleep (`farsleep`)" below). Classifies the object table once a session (leaf descendants of `Visual_Parent_obj`, `Destructible_NoCollision_Parent_obj`, `Collision_Prop_obj`, minus denied parents, `Trap_*` and objects owning per-frame events), scans a settled zone a slice a frame, then every 10 frames sleeps props beyond the sleep radius of every player (`instance_deactivate_object`) and wakes those inside a wake radius (`instance_activate_object`); a player jump starts an urgent pass. Reaches the game only through `CallBuiltin`, every call counted against a per-frame budget, so `tests/far_sleep_harness.cpp` compiles the real class against a controlled runner.
- `plugin_build/`: Plugin compiler script and build workspace.
  - `build.bat`: MSVC x64 batch script compiling `plugin/ModuleMain.cpp` into `BloodPactPlugin_ship.dll` (player build) or `BloodPactPlugin_rel.dll` (research build).
- `modfiles_shipped/`: Shipped binaries deployed to the game's `bin/` directory upon mod installation.
  - `AurieCore.dll`: Aurie Framework core loader binary (unmodified AGPL-3.0).
  - `AuriePatcher.exe`: Aurie PE import/bootstrap patcher (unmodified AGPL-3.0).
  - `YYToolkit.dll`: GameMaker runtime interface library, a modified build of YYToolkit v4.0.1 (AGPL-3.0). `origin`'s pin now names a build of the hub's patch series in `third_party/yytoolkit/` (sha256 `51a393d7…`, see the pin table below), merged via the pull request that also rewrote `yytoolkit-modified/`. The version this replaces was SHA-256 `bb113eef…` (904,192 bytes); its notice listed two changes — a runner-interface disk cache and the `ExecuteIt` hook left uninstalled — but the binary contained more than that, and the source it was built from was not kept; see the provenance row in the pin table below. No ForgePact release has shipped the new pin yet, so this is still the DLL a player's installed copy carries.
  - `BloodPactPlugin.dll`: Pre-compiled release build of the mod plugin.
  - `HSOfflineTrackerProducer.dll` (Optional): Read-only telemetry sensor for the companion HS Offline Tracker tool.
- `yytoolkit-modified/`: On `origin`, an AGPL-3.0 `NOTICE.md` pointing at the hub's [`third_party/yytoolkit/`](../../../third_party/yytoolkit/README.md) plus a `YYToolkit-BUILD-INFO.json` — the pull request that rewrote this directory to that pointer has merged, replacing the two whole-file copies it used to hold. **Not the complete source for the DLL, and not where a change to YYToolkit is made** — that is the hub's patch series. No ForgePact release has shipped the new pin yet, so a player's installed copy still carries the earlier DLL (SHA-256 `bb113eef…`) and the notice that accompanied it, which was incomplete.
  - `NOTICE.md`: Now a rationale and pointer at the hub series, not a rebuild recipe. The notice it replaced described a rebuild recipe for the two files below that said everything else was unmodified upstream, which the binary's own strings contradicted; its recipe (`MSVC toolset 14.50`, `cl /std:c++latest /MD /LD`) did not match the DLL's PE header (linker 14.51), and it omitted `/O2` although the DLL was **likely** built with it: a plain `cl /O2` build of the documented tree is 894,976 bytes against the DLL's 904,192, one without `/O2` is 1,180,160, and the DLL has pooled strings and no `.rsrc` section, unlike a build through upstream's `vcxproj`.
  - The two whole-file copies this directory used to hold, `Generic-RunnerInterfaceNew.cpp` and `source/YYTK/Hooks.cpp`, are gone from `origin`'s tree; they accompanied the previous, still-distributed DLL. `Generic-RunnerInterfaceNew.cpp` added a disk cache (`<exe>.yytkcache`) and a density-sorted `.text` page pre-filter so the RunnerInterface search did not disassemble all of `.text` on every launch — the pre-filter was in the file but never listed in `NOTICE.md`. On a cache hit that version planted the mid-function hook at the address read from the file, validating nothing but the executable's size — and `src/forgepact.py`'s `ensure_ri_cache` pre-seeded that file from a table of hand-measured RVAs keyed by exe size; that table has since been deleted (see Known Limitations). `source/YYTK/Hooks.cpp` left the `ExecuteIt` hook (`EVENT_OBJECT_CALL`) uninstalled; `EVENT_FRAME` via `HkPresent` was unaffected. The reason `NOTICE.md` gave — the hook corrupting instance references and producing `Unable to find any instance for object index` — was contradicted by `docs/S10-special-content-notes.md`, which records the same error with the hook removed. What stood: no plugin in this toolkit consumes `EVENT_OBJECT_CALL`, and project notes record the per-event hook crash-looping on Season 10 (not re-measured since). Both behaviours are now reproduced by the hub's patch series instead — `0001` keeps the exe-size hint but always takes the hook address from the scan, and `0003` makes the `ExecuteIt`-off state a named build switch, logs it once, and refuses the registration with `AURIE_UNAVAILABLE` instead of accepting a callback that can never fire.
- `tests/`: Automated Python contract test suites validating plugin source invariants and panel logic without requiring a running game instance.
  - `panel_source.py`: How a contract test reads the panel since the UI port. `PANEL_SRC` is `panel/src` (override: `FORGEPACT_TEST_PANEL_SRC`); `panel_source()` joins `App.svelte`, then `tabs/*.svelte`, then `lib/**`, then the remaining `*.js`/`*.css`/`*.svelte`, each after a `/* == <relative path> == */` line; `panel_file(rel)` reads one file; `js_for_node(text)` strips `export ` prefixes and `import …;` lines so a sliced function runs under `node`. Every test that used to read `forgepact.HTML` or `POLL_POLICY_JS` goes through it, keeping each fact it asserted. `FORGEPACT_TEST_PANEL_DIR` still means "the directory holding `forgepact.py`", for the Python-side facts.
  - `panel_sandbox_server.py`: The server the browser tooling drives (`[--legacy] [--dist <dir>] [--offline] [--satanic-minimum] [--seed <json>] [--src <dir>] [--start-delay <seconds>]`). `--src <dir>` imports `forgepact` from `<dir>` (an extracted `git archive <ref> src`) with the hub's `hs-game-sdk/python` first on `sys.path`, and refuses when the module it loaded is not that one; with `--legacy` it serves that ref's embedded page, which is how the Gems supplement is recorded. `--start-delay <seconds>` waits that long before anything is imported or the seed is read, so the port comes at least that much later on any machine: the late sandbox `test_start_sandbox_report.py` needs, since a start (about 0.5 s) is no longer slow enough to count on. It reuses `test_satanic_panel.PanelSandbox` (an isolated `forgepact.json` in a temp directory, served by `_SandboxServer`: forgepact's own server class and handler with a listen queue of 128 instead of socketserver's 5, so a browser's burst of connections waits while the process is slow to accept instead of being refused; see "Parallel load and the panel sandbox" under "The build half"), then reports the game as running so `/api/set` takes its live-send branches, appends every sent command to `<tmp>/cmds.txt`, and stubs install, remove, launch and the exe picker. Prints `port=<n>` and `cmds=<path>` and serves until stdin closes. The printed port is never one headless Edge refuses with `net::ERR_UNSAFE_PORT`: `PanelSandbox` re-binds off `CHROMIUM_RESTRICTED_PORTS` (see `test_sandbox_port.py` below) before handing out a port. `--legacy` points `PANEL_DIST` at a missing directory so the pre-port page is served (from this checkout only while that page still existed, for the recording; from any pre-port ref through `--src`); on a tree without the page it refuses with exit 2 and says why, instead of waiting on the 503. No route is added to the product for testing.
  - `test_sandbox_port.py`: No real socket - every bind is a fake object recording its calls on a shared event list. Pins `PanelSandbox`'s re-bind guard: a safe first port is kept after one bind (current behaviour); given a restricted port then a safe one, the helper (`bind_safe_server`) re-binds, keeping the rejected server open until the safe one is bound then closing it (closing first could hand the same port straight back on a machine whose OS hands out port-0 ports in sequence, e.g. `6665`-`6669`); exhausting `SANDBOX_BIND_ATTEMPTS` raises, naming the ports tried, after closing every server it bound; and `CHROMIUM_RESTRICTED_PORTS` holds the measured ports (`net/base/port_util.cc`'s `kRestrictedPorts` and the Fetch standard's bad-port list, checked against installed Edge) and not the panel's own port.
  - `test_sandbox_accept_queue.py`: Real loopback sockets, nothing served, so every connection stays queued as a starved accept loop leaves it. `_SandboxServer` holds a burst of 16 connections (more than the six headless Edge opens to one origin, plus a suite's own requests); on Windows forgepact's stock `ThreadingHTTPServer` refuses the same burst with `ConnectionRefusedError`, the instrument (Linux lets a connection wait instead, so that check runs on Windows only); `PanelSandbox` serves through `_SandboxServer`. With the class's queue put back to 5, the burst test fails on the refusal.
  - `test_start_sandbox_report.py`: Runs `startSandbox` under node (it needs node and `panel/node_modules`, not Edge or a build), and stops every sandbox the script starts. A seeded sandbox told to wait 10 s before it imports anything (`startDelayMs: 10000`, the server's `--start-delay`) and given `startTimeoutMs: 500` is rejected as "did not report its port within 0.5 s and was stopped", "stopped by SIGTERM", and never prints `--seed: cannot read`, which the same script's sandbox did about 10 s later when the timer gave up without the kill (the old behaviour, the negative control). The case used to count on a start taking seconds; since the SDK loads its tables on first use a start takes about 0.5 s (0.25 s without the SDK beside the checkout), so that sandbox could start in time, and nothing stopped it: node never exited, and the test waited out its 600 s `subprocess` timeout (2026-09-29). The delay makes the case late on any machine; the sandbox of the last case is given `startDelayMs: 1000` and must report its port no sooner than 1 s, the positive control that the delay reaches the server (with `startSandbox` not passing it, the control read 285-503 ms and a lost race failed in 2 s); and `StartDelayTests` pins that the server waits before `load()`, so before it reads its seed, without which "cannot read" could not catch a seed dropped too soon. A sandbox given an unknown `--seed` key rejects with "exited early", its exit code 2 and its own error line; that line reached the suite's stderr prefixed `[sandbox pid <n>]`; and after `stop()`, `state()` fails with `ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:<port>` and says the sandbox exited with code 0.
  - `test_panel_sdk_import.py`: The panel imports only `SATANIC_BUFFS` and `SATANIC_DEBUFFS` from `hs_game_sdk`, since a name from one of the SDK's generated tables builds that table at every start (Known Limitations item 38). An AST pin: both import statements, the plain one and the one after the `sys.path` fallback, name exactly those two. With `hs_game_sdk` made unimportable, the panel still imports and its pools are empty. In a fresh interpreter, `import forgepact` builds none of the five tables, with a positive control that imports `GameObject` after the panel and sees `objects` built; that class skips without an SDK, or with one from before hub #286 that builds every table on import. `FORGEPACT_TEST_PANEL_DIR` runs it against another tree: against `00d1a26`'s `forgepact.py` the pin and the table check fail. The hub's `tests/test_sdk_lazy_import.py` pins the same two names (`FORGEPACT_NAMES`).
  - `test_panel_browser_timeout.py`: `panel_browser._run_tree`, which `_npm` runs every suite through, ends the whole process tree at its timeout (`taskkill /T`), not only the process it started: given a child whose grandchild holds the same pipes and sleeps 120 s, a 3 s timeout returns in about 3 s, with the child's output and a line naming the timeout. `subprocess.run(timeout=...)`, which `_npm` used before, waits on such a grandchild for as long as it lives (20.2 s for a 20 s sleep, measured). It imports `panel_browser` as a module, not `from panel_browser import`, since that line is what marks a module that drives the browser suites.
  - `panel_browser.py`: Not a test module (discovery loads only `test_*.py`). The panel browser suites' shared skip check `_missing()` (names what is absent: `node`/`npm`, Edge, `panel/node_modules/playwright-core`, `panel/dist/index.html`) and `_npm(script)` (runs `npm --prefix panel run <script>`, 900 s timeout, returns exit code and output), imported by every module below that runs a suite rather than copied into each.
  - `test_panel_oracle_replay.py`, `test_panel_e2e.py`, `test_panel_e2e_gems.py`, `test_panel_e2e_polish.py`, `test_panel_e2e_motion.py`, `test_panel_e2e_finish.py`, `test_panel_e2e_review.py`, `test_panel_e2e_form.py`, `test_panel_e2e_ember_scroll.py`: One `npm run` suite each in `panel/` (`test_panel_e2e_review.py` runs two, `e2e:ember` and `e2e:review`) (`oracle:replay`, `e2e`, `e2e:gems`, `e2e:polish`, `e2e:motion` (`test_motion_e2e_suite_passes`, forgepact-ui-ship), `e2e:finish` (`test_finish_e2e_suite_passes`, the finish review's fixes), `e2e:review` and `e2e:ember` (ForgePact#105), `e2e:form` (2026-09-28; until then nothing ran it or `e2e:review`, see `test_panel_browser_coverage.py` below), `e2e:ember-scroll` (ForgePact#105)), asserting exit 0 and the suite's last line (`oracle: N steps, 0 mismatches`, or `n/n checks passed`); each skips, naming what is missing, per `panel_browser._missing()`. Each declares `PARALLEL_GROUP = "panel-browser"`, which `tools/run_tests_parallel.py` runs four at a time. They were one module, `test_panel_oracle.py`, until 2026-09-27, and since the runner parallelises by module the six suites then ran back to back on one worker: 735 s of a 1143 s run. Split, the six take about 259 s side by side, as long as `oracle:replay` alone. Measured times at a cap of 4: replay 259 s, polish 244 s, motion 158 s, finish 136 s, e2e 39 s, gems 37 s. `tests/test_run_tests_parallel.py` fails if a module that imports `panel_browser` is neither in that group nor exclusive.
  - `test_panel_browser_coverage.py`: Every `panel/tests/*.e2e.mjs` must be run by a `package.json` script that some `tests/test_*.py` declaring `PARALLEL_GROUP = "panel-browser"` calls through `panel_browser._npm(...)` (the pull-request workflow runs only that group, so an unmarked module does not count), or be listed in its `EXEMPT` with the reason CI cannot run it (today only `perf.e2e.mjs`, for `test_panel_perf.py`'s reason below). CI reaches a browser suite only through such a module, so a suite without one runs nowhere: `e2e:review` passed 28/28 on main and failed 15/28 on ForgePact#105 with nothing reporting it. It has a negative control (a throwaway tree with one wrapped and one unwrapped suite). **Adding a browser suite means adding its module**, copied from `test_panel_e2e_polish.py`.
  - `test_panel_oracle.py`: The behaviour-oracle recordings, with no browser (the replay itself is `test_panel_oracle_replay.py`). It pins `panel/tests/behaviour-oracle-gems.json`'s provenance: recorded from the legacy page at its `sourceRev`, its first **Save filter** (nothing ticked) posting and sending nothing and its second posting the two ticked mods, and the derived file naming it as `supplementFrom`. It pins `panel/tests/behaviour-oracle-primeevil.json`'s provenance the same way (recorded from the legacy page at `841c2db`, the slider's five actions between `tab:loot` and `#applyall`, its max step posting `{section: keys, key: primeevil, value: 100}` and sending `droprate group primeevil 100` between `colosfrag 1` and `ruby 1`, its min step the key at x1, and the derived file naming it as `keySupplementFrom`), and checks what `insertAddedKeys` adds against the merged backend: each of the 70 full key resets in `behaviour-oracle.json` equals `build_key_cmds(..., include_resets=True)` of that step's key values plus `primeevil` at 1, with the one line after `colosfrag`.
  - `test_panel_perf.py`: Runs `npm run e2e:perf` in `panel/` (`test_perf_e2e_suite_passes`, the hard budgets on medians of five runs, about six minutes) and asserts exit 0 and its `n/n` last line, with `panel_browser.py`'s skip conditions and npm helper (imported, not copied). It declares `PARALLEL_EXCLUSIVE = True`, so `tools/run_tests_parallel.py` runs it last and alone: under the parallel runner, sharing the CPU with native harness compiles and the other browser suites, the suite once measured one 71 ms frame on `budget-search-controls` while the same suite alone passed 26/26 on the same tree (forgepact-ui-ship round 2), which measured the machine, not the panel. The serial run is unaffected.
  - `test_panel_packaging.py`: Pins `PANEL_DIST`'s three sources (frozen, source, environment), the path-traversal refusal, the suffix-to-MIME table, the 503 on a missing build, the absence of the legacy page string, and `build_release.py`'s `panel/dist` guard and `--add-data`.
  - `test_slider_switches.py`: The slider switches and the theme key on the backend. Baselines first: `build_cmds()` for a config with non-default sliders in every section (plus rarity, angelic and enemy speed) as a literal list, and the live `/api/set` lines for one slider per section at `min` and `max` through `PanelSandbox` with the game reported running. Then the targets: `DEFAULTS` carries `switches` and `theme`; `SLIDER_SWITCH_IDS` covers every slider but density; `build_cmds()` is unchanged with every switch on and emits the default with a switch off; turning a switch off live sends the slider's own default command and turning it on sends the remembered value; a slider moved while off saves its value and sends the default; an unknown switch id and an invalid theme name are refused.
  - `test_enabled_mods_panel.py`: Source contracts for the enabled-mods list, the switches and the theme select, read through `panel_source.py`: the list's strings are exactly the fixed copy (`Enabled mods`, `<n> on`, `Nothing is on`, `Turn off`, and the `Turn off <name>`/`Enable <row label>` aria-labels); entries and `Turn off` buttons carry `data-for`; the list's files name no `/api` route; the `/api/set` call sites are the port's plus exactly the switch and theme handlers; every slider row has a switch and density keeps `#den_on`; the list sits under `.control-dock`; the `#theme` select exists in `tabs/Setup.svelte`'s Appearance card (not in the status bar) and the root carries `data-theme`.
  - `panel_ui_browser.js`, `satanic_panel_browser.js`, `panel_install_browser.js`, `panel_launcher_browser.js`: **Removed in the UI port.** They were `agent-browser eval --stdin` harnesses against `PanelSandbox` that no suite ran; every check they made is in `panel/tests/ui.e2e.mjs` under the same label.
  - `test_density_reentry_contract.py`: Validates spawner object name lookups, stable spatial identity keys, and density placement guards.
  - `test_enemy_speed_contract.py`: Verifies enemy speed multipliers, Chaos Tower scoping, and release command accessibility.
  - `test_kill_drop_contract.py`: Pins where the angelic and signature kill drops run inside `Hook_EnemyDestroyKillProc` - both before the original kill proc, handed the live enemy, inside a catch-all, and nothing naming `S` after the trampoline (Known Limitations item 14). The behaviour itself is exercised by the drop scenarios in `test_headhunter_dispatch.py` + `headhunter_dispatch_harness.cpp`, which compile the real drop functions and skip without a C++ toolchain. Since #63 (Known Limitations item 21) that dispatch test's kill-drop scenarios split into `test_signature_drop_baseline` (the seven kill-order scenarios listed in Known Limitations item 14 - must still pass against the pre-#63 source) and `test_signature_drop_target` (`signature_pool_append`, `angelic_pick_crown_spawns_signature`, `angelic_pick_belt_spawns_signature`, `signature_equal_share`, `sigdrop_force_belt`, `sigdrop_force_no_alternation` - the pool-membership behaviour, each shown failing against the pre-#63 source).
  - `test_signature_drop_contract.py`: Source contract for Headhunter/Tyrant's Crown joining the Angelic pool (#63) - pins that the six standalone-die identifiers (`kSigDropAngelicPct`, `g_SigDropPct`, `g_SigDropAncientPct`, `g_SigDropPity`, `g_SigDropSinceLast`, `g_SigDropNext`) are gone, that `BuildAngelicPool`'s body calls `AppendSignatureCandidates(`, that `AngelicDropOnKill`'s body calls `SpawnSignatureItem(`, and that the panel's `angelicCard` hint names both items. Honours `FORGEPACT_TEST_PLUGIN_SOURCE` like the dispatch test, so it fails against the pre-#63 source.
  - `test_forge_hash_contract.py`: Validates the Custom Forge's `itemDataHash` refresh chain (`RefreshItemHash`, with `+itemcheck`/`+method`/`+direct` split into named `HashRouteItemCheck`/`HashRouteMethod`/`HashRouteDirect` helpers and `+routine` kept inline in `RefreshItemHash` itself behind a `routineOnly` flag, so its `AddrIsExecutableInModule` guard stays where `test_release_hook_contract.py` pins it) - the SDK-constant fix for the stale `@anon@4638@` script name, `+direct`'s before/after hash compare and its `+direct-nohash` refusal, that the route helpers never touch the shared counters, that `hashprobe`'s single-route line leads with the `wrote=` verdict rather than the route's own (misleading, post-sentinel) return, that its no-write restore uses the raw original `RValue` rather than a reconstructed string, and that `hashprobe`/`forgehash stat` (both research-build only, per Known Limitations item 15) stay out of `kPlayerCommands` and out of a release build entirely.
  - `test_mod_backup.py`: Tests clean PE backup verification, build mismatch detection, stale backup archival, and atomic restore rollbacks.
  - `test_necro_balance_contract.py`: Validates Necromancer balance formulas, fail-closed runtime contracts, and panel visibility.
  - `test_map_reveal_contract.py`: Validates the map-reveal mod and its `map_reveal_packs` sub-toggle - defaults, `build_cmds` parent/child emission, the release-guarded `reveal stat`, and the guardrails that keep it from unlocking waypoints, writing the player's minimap options, or sweeping `isDiscovered`. Also pins the 2026-09-11 regression: the pack pass must wait for a creator to report a real `enemyCreatorTimer` before lying about distance, because firing during zone load leaves the spawners inert and the zone emptier than vanilla.
  - `test_map_reveal_behavior.py` + `map_reveal_harness.cpp`: **Behavioral** regression suite for the pack pass - compiles the real `MapRevealManager` and the real `Hook_distance_to_object` against controlled game-API responses and calls the hook at the point in the frame order where it matters (before the next `OnFrame`). Exists because the source-string assertions in `test_map_reveal_contract.py` passed throughout the period when the authorization was checked at the wrong point in the frame; see Known Limitations item 13. Skips without a C++ toolchain, like `test_headhunter_dispatch.py`.
  - `test_frame_profiler.py` + `frame_profiler_harness.cpp`: The frame profiler. **Behaviour** (Windows, skips without MSVC): the harness compiles the real `FrameProfiler.hpp` and profiles a worker thread of its own that runs a known chain (`FpTop` -> `FpMiddle` -> `FpLeafSpin`) named through a fake compiled-code table (`gml_Object_Fake_Enemy_obj_Step_0`, `gml_Script_FakeMiddle`) and a fake built-in (`fake_spin`), calling `OnFrame` at its own frame boundaries. The JSON reports must name the event, the script and the built-in (>= 60% each), put the script first in `gmlSelf`, read a sleeping thread as idle, pin every slow frame on `FakeSlowWork`, carry the context callback's room and counts into the timeline, list stacks outermost first, end early on `stop` and on a vanished thread, cut the sampling rate when the pauses exceed the budget, count a frame limiter that spins on the clock (`FpFrameLimiter`, in no table) as waiting while a clock read under game code never is, and keep naming a row whose function a "mod" swapped for a function in another module (`FpMiddle`'s row pointed at `SleepEx`), read back from the harness exe on disk - the reason the fake table is a constant-initialised `FakeRow` array with typed function pointers. The heap-churn capture (2000 a second against a thread allocating in a loop) must finish: a lock taken while the thread is suspended would deadlock it, and the harness's watchdog ends the process if it ever does. Compiled with `/link /INCREMENTAL:NO` so a function's address is the function, not a thunk. **Contract**: `frameprof` is in `kPlayerCommands` and a standalone early return (the literal twice in the file); `FrameProfilerTick();` is `FrameCallback`'s first statement; `OnFrame` and `TakeSummary` start with one atomic load; the code between `SuspendThread(` and `ResumeThread(` in `CaptureOnce` holds no allocation, logging, lock, unwinder call or throw, and `WalkCopy` comes after it; `RtlLookupFunctionEntry(` never appears; the header names no `RValue`/`g_Yytk`/`YYTK`/`CInstance`/`Aurie`, no `std::min`/`std::max`; the adapter installs no hook and calls only `room_get_name`, `asset_get_index`, `instance_number` and reads `room` and `instance_count`; the defaults are 30 s at 250 a second.
  - `test_frameprof_report.py`: `tools/frameprof_report.py` on a synthetic report and stacks file: the call tree adds up, labels pick their colour (event, script, built-in, runtime, graphics, mod, system), the summary lines, a hostile name is escaped and every section is on the page with nothing loaded from the network, a second without a monster reading is left out of the chart, a non-capture JSON is refused, and without a path the newest capture of the game in `forgepact.json` is taken.
  - `test_far_sleep_behavior.py` + `far_sleep_harness.cpp`: Far scenery sleep. The harness compiles the real `FarSleep.hpp` against a runner with an object table (the three families, the denied parents, a trap, an object with a Step event, a gap in the indices) and active/asleep instances; 38 scenarios: nothing asked while off, bounded runner calls, only scenery, settling, walking, jumps, two players, the hunt radius, a prop that moved, a prop broken while awake, the game waking props, the top-up scan, off, room changes, skipped rooms, a restart, a refusing runner.
  - `test_far_sleep_contract.py`: The wiring and the rules: `farsleep` is a player command with its own early return, `FrameCallback` runs `FarSleepTick()` after setup and the tick returns at once while off, the class starts off and calls only `CallBuiltin` (and only the documented built-ins), the families, the denied parents, the skipped rooms, and that `zonecensus`, `evcount` and `farsleep ids` stay in the research build.
  - `test_rolling_density_contract.py`: Rolling density copies (`densityroll`, see "Rolling density copies (`densityroll`)" below): the player command and its early return, off at start, the budget seeing only the due copies, the pack markers told only while rolling, the reach rules (fill pass, hunts), the command's 1,500-20,000 px range, the modstate keys, the panel's `density_rolling` default and live `densityroll 1|0`, and the README row, section and 2.1.0 notes (the notes check skips once the cleanup workflow deletes them). The behaviour lives in `adaptive_population.cpp`, `density_population_harness.cpp` (`test_adaptive_population.py`) and `pack_markers_harness.cpp` (`copy/...`).
  - `test_menu_probe_contract.py`: Pins the research stage of the character-select question - whether anything can drive the game's own main menu as far as a loaded character (`docs/character-select-research.md`; live session run 2026-09-21).
    - `menuprobe` is research-build only (the literal disappears from `strip_research_blocks`, header comment included), absent from `kPlayerCommands`, dispatched from `HandleMenuProbeCommand` as the third adjacent call after the Headhunter's and the prospect window's rather than a new top-level `else if` (C1061; the chain's length is pinned against `f5a3515`), and the literal occurs exactly once in `ModuleMain.cpp`.

  - `test_stash_move_all_contract.py`: Pins #68's player build on comment-stripped source: both verbs in `kPlayerCommands`, each from its own `Handle*` helper; every routine an SDK constant through one by-name dispatcher (`SmaCall`); no write builtin and nothing by address; the frame path returning at once while off and asking the foreground and the stash only while F4 is down; each item re-read (bag cell, map 0, the tab on show and its room) before its first call and decided from the re-reads after it; the loss rule and the undo's condition; the button block (`UiCreateNode`/`UiRemoveNode` by SDK constant, self the stash window, Sort found by `uiNodeCallstack` `InventorySort`, the activation undefined, no `UiSetActivationFunc` and no script hook in the player build, the label the only write, the press the frame poll handed to the core, removal on off, a loss and the off tick); the Socketable tab read as its `StashSocketGrid` nodes, never `stashSocketItemSlot`; the panel's `mod_stash_move_all` (off by default, `stashmoveall 1|0`) and its short player text; the README's and the 2.1.0 notes' full-tab sentence and the button; the research doc's `## Ship design` and `### Live 2 results`.

The fake `DropMonsterGold` original calls the *hooked* `DropGold` once, the way the inline detour sees the game's direct call. Baselines: the other drop targets keep their count semantics (x3 runs the original three times), and one hooked `DropMonsterGold` at x100 runs each gold original once - the documented "before", which printed `monster_originals=100 gold_originals=10000` run red against the old header. Skips without a C++ toolchain, like `test_orb_pickup_behavior.py`.

    - `resize_restore_never_exceeds_the_store` (the restore is capped per axis at what `nodeGrid` still covers and prints `restore unsafe` when that is below vanilla; a method that replaced the node prints `rebuilt`/`destroyed`);
    - `call_and_resize_prove_the_method_body_ran` (`script_execute` succeeding proves the dispatch, not the body: the closure's own detoured row is counted across the invoke and `invoked=yes|NO|unproven`, `self=` and `args=` ride on every outcome line; `resize` takes trailing numeric arguments);
    - `setat_requires_a_detoured_row_and_an_existing_variable`;
    - and the doc tests `research_doc_records_phase0a_as_instrument_failure`, `research_doc_closure_rows_have_a_positive_control` (L4: the four closures read live - `anon@1065/2806/3657/36159` - must print `detoured`; `detoured` proves resolution only, a count needs `invoked=yes`), `research_doc_pins_the_three_sentences` (the third: a `CHANGED` snapshot names the builder's extent, not the builder) and `research_doc_pins_the_resize_revert_rule` (a `reverted` counts toward H3 only with `invoked=yes` and the call shape L5 logged for the game's own call, else `not observed (call shape unknown)`; and only on a grow - GML's element assignment grows an array and never truncates it, so a shrink's `reverted` is `not observed (shrink only - an assignment-built store never truncates)`; L9 shrinks every method first, then grows every method whose shrink kept **and** every method whose shrink reverted with `invoked=yes` and an unchanged store, arms the method's row before each `resize` so the detour logs what it received, and voids every `resize` of the session if the closure-name matcher prints `invoked=unproven (no detoured row …)` for a detoured row; L5's "no `CHANGED`" needs a resolved `grid-post`).

    - `idcheck_runs_a_positive_control_before_its_verdict` (on arrays the instrument builds itself, a kept reference must see a later write and the scanner must find the sentinel through it but not in a separate array - both before `nodeGrid` is touched, returning on failure - and `copy` needs a complete walk, else `scan incomplete`; a reference, method or pointer counts as unwalked, and a kept return of a profile getter (`GetProfileInventoryData`/`GetPlayerProfileObj`) holding the sentinel decides `reference-identical (via ...)`, a non-profile getter’s hit only a lead);
    - `idcheck_a_profile_getter_hit_from_one_call_only_is_a_lead` (a profile getter’s CLEAN hits - paths through no UI-looking field - must span `kPpBackingProfileCallsToDecide` (two) distinct calls to decide save-backed; each verdict literal is pinned to its own branch of the collapsed verdict body by slicing on `if (decisive) {`/`} else if (uiReached) {`/`} else {`, not just to text order; the decisive/uiReached selection block is pinned whole after comment-stripping and whitespace-collapsing, none of the five names it reads - the stash list, the threshold, `PpBackingIsProfileGetter` and the two call maps - may be redeclared, rebound or `#define`d (the maps are declared once, above it), and an in-file, in-memory mutation control proves the pin rejects 23 keyword-free restrictions that passed the earlier asserts, M24's first-stash wrap among them);
    - `idcheck_a_decisive_identity_through_a_ui_looking_field_is_a_lead` (`PpBackingUiLookingField` classifies a hit path’s struct-member names against `ui`/`window`/`node`/`panel`/`menu`, case-insensitive; a profile getter whose hits reach the two-call threshold only through such a field prints a `reached through a UI-looking field` lead instead of deciding save-backed, pinned in the doc’s § Instrument, C5, the gate’s Save-backed/Inconclusive bullets and the § Results row);
    - `idcheck_one_call_only_lead_names_every_profile_getter_that_hit` (the `one call only` lead names every profile getter whose kept returns held the sentinel, not just the first in stash order);
    - `idcheck_refuses_unless_a_kept_return_came_from_the_open_window` (window-self returns are kept with their `@id`, an unreadable id never matches, the open-window refusal comes before the one write, and the live procedure runs `idcheck` (C3) before C2b places any item);
