The boss-room outlier was not run (the owner's choice, "no need"); round 3 fought in `Town_01_rm` only.
    - **Research probe and mod in one session (not run live).** From a static reading of `HookOneScript` and `RestartProbeAttach`: the mod hooks `UiSetFocus` through both routes, so its saved original is a trampoline and the table entry is our own hook, neither of them code inside `Hero_Siege.exe` - which is why the probe's `UiSetFocus` row stays unbound (unlike `ZoneGenRestart`, which attaches under table-only `zonegenlog`). With the mod's hook in first, a later `restartprobe hook` reports that one row `blocked` and attaches the other 21. With `restartprobe hook` first, the mod's install asks the hooking library to detour an address the probe already patched; `docs/prospect-window-research.md` says the second hook on an address fails, measured only in the other order, so this is unverified - if it fails, the mod logs `restartanytime: hook TABLE-ONLY -> OFF` and stays off for the session. Neither order was run. Run the probe or the mod in a session, not both. Players never meet either case: the player build has no probe (`restartprobe` is behind `#ifndef FORGEPACT_RELEASE`).
    - **Tests:** `tests/test_restart_anytime_contract.py` (`RestartAnytimeContractTests`, `RestartResearchDocTests`), `tests/test_restart_anytime_behavior.py` + `restart_anytime_harness.cpp`.
23. **Half of the Relics slider does nothing observable: `droprate group relic` divides every relic base by the same factor, and `DropRelic`'s drop is not a 1-in-base roll (recorded 2026-09-24, issue #162):**
    - **What is measured.** `DropRelic`'s drop is not a 1-in-`droprate.base` roll: all 156 relics carried 25,000,000 and relics still dropped constantly (measured 2026-08-28, ForgePact `c0a6a6b`; not re-measured since). `build_key_cmds` still sends `droprate group relic <m>` with every Relics setting, and the plugin's `relic` branch of `DropRateCmd` still divides all relic bases by the same factor, so that half of the slider writes values for which no reader was observed. Whether `DropRelic` reads the base some other way, for example as a weight in the pick between relics or when reached from one of its other direct callers, is not established: every relic had the same value, a uniform divide leaves such a pick unchanged, and no run changed a single relic's base. So do not take the field as dead when weighting relics per item. The slider's real effect is the other half: the type-41 gate through `dungeonkey`, thinned by the quadratic pre-roll (`g_DkTipOlcek`).
    - **Answered by a static reading (2026-09-30, #125).** No relic pick reads `droprate.base`: all three routines that place a relic draw `irandom(155)` and draw again while `GetRelicQuest` answers true, so every non-quest relic is equally likely whatever its base (hub `docs/models/relic-pick-spec.md`). The base write stays harmless; it is simply read by no pick.
    - **Harmless, not removed.** The write is cheap and the relic bases are restored at x1. Removing the command is a separate ForgePact change; this entry only records it.
    - **Where it is checked.** The hub's `tests/test_drop_roll_model.py` (`TargetTests.test_m7_relic_base_lever_is_a_no_op`) shows the lever writing a relic base that the modelled relic roll cannot read, and `docs/models/drop-roll-spec.md` records the mechanism.
24. **"Craft from the stash" (`craftmats`, issue #14) moves the stash's materials and saves the stash at the craft press, a moment the game did not choose - an accepted risk (2026-09-24):**
    - **What it does.** With the switch on, a Crafting Cube recipe's count gains the stash's Materials and Socketable tabs, only inside the game's own crafting route (the availability call, the recipe row's Create closure, and `CraftFindRecipeItems` at the press); outside it every count is the game's own. At the press, only the amount the bag is short of moves from those tabs by the game's own routines - onto the bag's stack, into a new bag stack, or into the Cube's `craftGrid` - before the game's own `DoCraftResult`, and the stash is saved by the game's own route (`SaveLocalFile(4, 1)`) right after. Design and every call shape: `ForgePact/docs/crafting-materials-research.md` `## Ship design`.
    - **The accepted risk.** Items move, and the stash is saved, at a moment the game did not choose. Quoting the owner's answer to the build's risk question (2026-09-24): "accept". The game's own craft can produce with nothing consumed (the vanilla duplication, `### Constraints from Phase 1`), so the mod never lets the stash supply an input it did not count and move: a press whose moves cannot all be confirmed is refused before the game's call (`craftmats: ... the craft was refused`), and a craft whose consume does not match what moved turns the mod off for the session (`craftmats: consume mismatch - ...`). The count is injected only inside the crafting route.
    - **Two additions the owner accepted after they were explained (2026-09-24, "Accept").** The decode hook sits on a hot game function, `PilipaliDecrypt` (672,588 calls in one research session): outside the `CraftFindRecipeItems` frame its body only forwards, but it is on the game's path for as long as the hooks are installed, off or on. And after a press-time stash save the character's own save is left to the game: a crash between the two loses that craft's stash-supplied materials.
    - **What is not observed live** (the owner's choice of two Phase C cases, "Keep two cases"): the Cube fallback (a full bag), a whole-entry take (a shortfall that empties a stash stack), and the game's own hash acceptance of an edited or created item beyond Phase C's watch for a flagged item. The undo shapes a failed take runs (`RemoveItemFromMap` on map 0, `GridRemoveItem` on a bag or Cube grid, `AddItemToMap` on map 9) reuse measured scripts on another map or grid and were not run live.
    - **Research build and mod in one session.** `craftmats 1` refuses while `craftprobe hook` already detours any of the six scripts, and `craftprobe hook` reports them `held by craftmats` once the mod's hooks are in - a second detour on one function would fail and read as a false `TABLE-ONLY`. Players never meet either: the player build has no `craftprobe`.
    - **Live confirmation:** Phase C, 2026-09-24, on the player DLL `BloodPactPlugin_ship.dll`, sha256 `eedc27c30c57236ecbf0e1dd8c04a423e257aaea927911e6e9d932edb01c46f3` (built from ForgePact `6f45abe`), slot 14, two launches. 13 of its 14 checks pass: the DLL and the player build's command set, the six hooks installed `both-routes`, a Socketable recipe (Ol, 2 moved onto the bag's own stack) and a Materials recipe (Greater Unstable Dust, 5 moved into a new bag stack) each unavailable with the switch off and crafting once with it on, one result per press, the stash saved after each press, and the lowered counts in the stash window, after the game's own quit and reload, and with the game stopped, with no flagged item observed. `bag-control` fails on the produced item's name only: a recipe the bag covered produced a Satanic Crystal Fragment in place of a Destiny Shard Fragment, while its count and the absent move line held and the mod did not touch that craft; the owner accepted it as an unrelated game bug ("Accept as game bug"). The owner added two cases, each observed live once: a multi-input recipe (Nut: 3 Sal and 1 Chipped Sapphire, both from the stash) and a multi-unit press of it (quantity 3: 9 and 3 moved in one line, 3 Nuts). The Cube's recipe list shows availability as computed when the Cube opened: after `craftmats 0` a recipe the stash had made available still read available while the window stayed open, and read unavailable at the next Cube open (observed once, on to off); a press on a row whose shown availability is stale was not observed. Record: `ForgePact/docs/crafting-materials-research.md` `## Phase C results`.
    - **Tests:** `tests/test_craft_mats_contract.py` (the `test_craftmats_*`, panel and research-doc tests), `tests/test_craft_mats_behavior.py` + `craft_mats_harness.cpp`.
25. **The shipped HS-Offline-Tracker producer turns some game exits into a WER crash report (measured from crash dumps, 2026-09-26):**
    - **What a player sees.** At or after closing the game, Windows reports Hero Siege crashed: faulting module `ucrtbase.dll`, exception `0xc0000409`, fast-fail parameter 7 (`abort`). A dump lands in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CrashDumps`.
    - **What it is.** `modfiles_shipped/HSOfflineTrackerProducer.dll` (pinned from the 1.3.16 package, a 2026-09-07-or-earlier build) keeps its publisher in a global `std::thread` (`g_publish_worker` in HS-Offline-Tracker's `aurie-producer/src/module.cpp`) and joins it only in `StopPublishWorker`. When the game leaves through `ExitProcess`, its other threads are gone before the DLL's globals are destroyed, and a `std::thread` still joinable at destruction calls `std::terminate`. The dumps' thread is inside `LdrShutdownProcess`, in that DLL's exit-time destructors, with the game's ordinary exit path under it.
    - **How often.** Most likely on every exit once the producer's publisher runs. 9 of the 10 dumps Windows kept (2026-09-25 13:17 to 2026-09-26 01:36) show it, one from a session that lived 70 s. HS-Offline-Tracker PR #8's check, launched with `CREATE_DEFAULT_ERROR_MODE`, saw it on a plain `CloseMainWindow` at the main menu (exit `0xC0000409`, dump 56756). The five `CloseMainWindow` closes of 2026-09-26 that left no dump were games started by Python under Git Bash. That Python's error mode is `0x3` (`SEM_NOGPFAULTERRORBOX`, measured), and the games inherited it, so Windows did not report their exits. Their exit codes were not read. `tools/itemtruth_memrun.py` now starts the game with the default error mode and reads the exit code (ForgePact PR #97).
    - **A missing dump is not a clean exit (measured 2026-09-26, item 26's live check).** That check's first `deaf068` run was also started by Python under Git Bash and inherited error mode `0x3`. It aborted with exit code `0xC0000409`, yet Windows wrote no dump and logged no Application Error event. The same run started with `CREATE_DEFAULT_ERROR_MODE` dumped. PowerShell resets its own error mode to `0x8001`, without `SEM_NOGPFAULTERRORBOX`. Judge a close by the game's exit code, not by the dump folder.
    - **Why it matters here.** The session of 197,704 Item Truth evaluations that "crashed" at 01:36:27 is one of them; it was read as the evaluations running out of memory (they do not: "Item Truth for the Item Editor", Memory). Read a dump's stack before blaming a `ucrtbase` report on ForgePact or the game.
    - **Not fixed here.** The fix belongs in HS-Offline-Tracker (join or detach the worker before the globals go); ForgePact then updates the pin. `ItemTruth.hpp` avoids the same trap for its own writer thread by never destroying its `Journal`; ForgePact's own coop receive thread did not, until item 26.
26. **ForgePact's own coop receive thread aborted research-build exits the same way (fixed in ForgePact PR #90; verified live 2026-09-26):**
    - **What it was.** `plugin/ModuleMain.cpp` kept the custom co-op transport's receive thread in `static std::thread g_CoopRecvThread`. `coopstart`, or a `bp_ipc\coop.ini` with `enabled=1` (read on the first frame), started it, and only `coopstop` joined it. Closing the game with coop still running therefore ended in item 25's abort, from ForgePact's own exit-time destructors.
    - **Who could meet it.** Research builds only. No preprocessor guard keeps the coop code out of the player build, but no `coop*` verb is in `kPlayerCommands`, so `RunCommand` refuses them there, and the `coop.ini` auto-start and the per-frame `CoopTick` are inside `#ifndef FORGEPACT_RELEASE`.
    - **The fix.** `plugin/include/ForgePact/ExitSafeThread.hpp`: the `std::thread` lives on the heap in a trivially destructible holder and is freed only after a join, so nothing runs for it at exit. It has the same shape as HS-Offline-Tracker PR #8's fix. `coopstop` closes the socket and waits at most 2 s (`kCoopStopJoinTimeout`) where it used to join without a bound on the frame thread. A thread that does not end is kept, and `coopstart` refuses until a later `coopstop` has joined it.
    - **Not covered: an Aurie unload.** ForgePact exports no `ModuleUnload` and never restores the script-table entries `HookOneScript` writes, so the Aurie console's "Unload framework" leaves them pointing into an unmapped module with or without coop (static reading, not run). The holder changes nothing there: a receive thread still running when Aurie calls `FreeLibrary` is left in unmapped code, where the old shape aborted instead.
    - **Tests.** `tests/test_coop_thread_exit_behavior.py` (with `coop_thread_exit_probe.cpp`, a DLL, and `coop_thread_exit_harness.cpp`) ends child processes with `ExitProcess`: the old static `std::thread` aborts (the baseline, caught as exit `0x7E2` so no dump is written), `ExitSafeThread` exits 0 (the target), and three cases cover `coopstop`. `tests/test_coop_thread_exit_contract.py` pins the wiring, the bound and the reachability above, and fails on any new named `std::thread` in the plugin that its `NAMED_THREADS` table does not list with a reason. Today that table lists only the Item Truth `Journal`'s thread.
    - **Live check (2026-09-26, at the owner's request).** Two research builds were run: `deaf068` (before #90) and `9915ca5` (#90 merged).
      - **Procedure.** Each build was launched to the main menu and ran `coopstart 47801 127.0.0.1 47802` (reply `coop: started`). Then `CloseMainWindow` at the main menu, the close that left dump 19000 at 10:38 that day (a producer abort, by its stack). `HSOfflineTrackerProducer.dll` was moved out of `mods\aurie` for these runs, so its own abort could not be the one observed, and then put back (SHA-256 checked).
      - **Result.** `deaf068` exited `0xC0000409` (abort); `9915ca5` exited `0`. A fast fail sets `0xC0000409` whatever the error mode, so the `9915ca5` exit is conclusive.
      - **Attribution.** The first `deaf068` run left no dump (item 25, "A missing dump is not a clean exit"). A second `deaf068` run, launched with `CREATE_DEFAULT_ERROR_MODE`, wrote `Hero_Siege.exe.25484.dmp`. Its stack is `ucrtbase!abort` ← `terminate` ← `BloodPactPlugin.dll+0x3F7823` ← `_execute_onexit_table`. That frame is inside the 25-byte function at RVA `0x3F7810`, whose address the DLL hands to `atexit`: the static `std::thread`'s destructor. The `9915ca5` DLL has no such function; the shipped producer has the same one at `0x1A9F0`.
27. **The panel's theme and slider switches live in `forgepact.json`, not in the browser, and an off slider reads `off` (2026-09-25):**
    - **Where they are kept.** The theme name (`theme`) and every slider switch that is off (`switches`) are saved through `/api/set` into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Hero_Siege\forgepact.json`, like every other setting (Data Formats § 1). Nothing in `panel/src/` uses `localStorage`: the desktop window is pywebview, whose web storage is private to it and not the same store a browser pointed at `http://127.0.0.1:8780` sees, so a browser-side setting would differ between the two front ends and would not travel with the rest of the config.
    - **An off slider's value box reads `off`**, with class `val off`, as Monster Density's always has; the slider keeps its saved value and stays movable, and turning the switch back on sends that value. The number is not shown while off until the visual restyle decides how to draw it (owner, 2026-09-25: no new string before then).
    - **Satanic Zone Mods are not entries in the `Enabled mods` list** (owner, 2026-09-25): the pool is all-on by default, so an enabled modifier is the default state rather than a mod the player turned on, and the World tab's own Enabled/Disabled filter already lists them.
28. **The panel bundles IBM Plex Sans and IBM Plex Mono, and preloads them (2026-09-25; used since the restyle, preloaded since forgepact-ui-ship):**
    - **What ships.** `panel/src/fonts/` holds the two upstream upright variable `.woff2` files of https://github.com/IBM/plex, `IBM Plex Sans Var-Roman.woff2` from tag `@ibm/plex-sans-variable@0.2.0` and `IBM Plex Mono Var-Roman.woff2` from tag `@ibm/plex-mono-variable@1.0.0` (stored as `IBMPlexSans/IBMPlexSans-Variable.woff2` and `IBMPlexMono/IBMPlexMono-Variable.woff2`), byte-identical to those tags, each beside its `LICENSE.txt` (SIL Open Font License 1.1, the `license.txt` upstream ships beside the fonts); `CREDITS.md` names them. Both files carry a `wght` axis of 100 to 700 (Sans also a `wdth` axis of 85 to 100, which `fonts.css` does not declare). `panel/src/fonts.css` declares the two faces and Vite bundles both files into `panel/dist/`, so they are inside the panel `ForgePact.exe` carries (about 314 KB) and nothing is loaded from the network. They replaced the typeface bundled earlier the same day, when the owner changed it.
    - **Used and preloaded.** Since the restyle, `tokens.css` names them as `--font-body-family` and `--font-mono-family`, so the whole panel draws in them. `fonts.css` keeps `font-display: block` (`swap` would flash the fallback face first), and `panel/index.html` preloads both files with `<link rel="preload" as="font" type="font/woff2" crossorigin>` (owner, 2026-09-26), which Vite rewrites to the same hashed `/assets/*.woff2` the built CSS names; `crossorigin` is required, because fonts are fetched in CORS mode and a preload made without it is not reused. `fonts.css`'s header comment says the same (corrected in the finish review's round). `panel/tests/fonts-load.mjs` still loads each face explicitly to prove the files decode.
    - **No italic** is bundled. If the design ever asks for an italic style, it has to be added here first.
29. **`talentalloc` (toolkit issue #147) changes the character's talents through the game's own handlers at a moment the game did not choose - an accepted risk (2026-09-25):**
    - **What it does.** A tool such as the hub's `hs_talent_allocate` sends it; the panel never does, and it has no switch. It opens the talent screen by name if it is closed and runs the talent's (or a sub-talent node's) own activation script with the real button as self, so the game does its own bookkeeping and its own hashing of the talent state - a raw write would trip that hash, which is why none is made. Design and every call shape: `ForgePact/docs/skill-actions-research.md` § Decision (`allocRoute`, `subAllocRoute`, `talentScreenOpenRoute`).
    - **The accepted risk.** The allocation happens outside a click the game saw. Live 3's W6 measured that the game's save accepts a by-name allocation, and its sub-node, across a stop, launch and reload (2026-09-26): both were still present, and the newly learned talent had filled a bar slot by itself. Quoting the owner's answer to the risk question (2026-09-25): "Accept" - for `talentalloc` and its sub-allocation, the verbs live 2 made shippable.
    - **What it cannot confirm.** No reader for the points left or a talent's level was found (`pointsReader: not measured`; `ReturnTalentLevel` by name threw), so the verb confirms by what live 2 measured changing: the id joining `global.mySkills`, or one `s<NN>` of the talent's `global.subTalentMap[1]` node rising by one. It refuses a talent already learned (a second level was never measured), and it cannot tell "no free point" from any other refusal of the game's own - both read `not confirmed`.
    - **What it does not undo.** No by-name undo exists (the right-click undo closures are not reproducible by name), so a test that allocates restores its save backup afterwards, as the hub's tools require one taken before the launch.
    - **Not built:** a bind to a bar slot and a reset of the tree (`shape not reproduced` in live 2).
    - **Tests:** `tests/test_skill_actions_contract.py` (`PlayerVerbs`); the hub's `tests/test_hs_drive_mcp_skills.py`.
30. **The stash and bag verbs `playerwarp`, `stashtab`, `bagtab`, `stashclose` and `giveitem` (toolkit issue #147) change game state through the game's own routines at a moment the game did not choose, and `giveitem` creates an item the game did not drop - an accepted risk (2026-09-25):**
    - **What they do.** A tool such as the hub's `hs_stash_open`, `hs_stash_tab`, `hs_bag_tab`, `hs_stash_close` or `hs_give_item` sends them; the panel never does, and none has a switch. `playerwarp` writes the player's room position by name; `stashtab` and `bagtab` run a tab's own handler by name with the self and other live 2 supplied; `stashclose` runs the close button's own handler, which is the route that saves the stash; `giveitem` makes a copy of an item the character already holds by the game's own loader (craftmats' proven json route, into the bag only), with a fresh key and timestamp, so the game builds and hashes it the way a load builds one - a raw write would trip the grid handlers' hash checks, which is why none is made. Design and every call shape: `ForgePact/docs/stash-bag-layout-research.md` § Decision.
    - **The accepted risk.** Quoting the owner's answers to the two risk questions (D16, 2026-09-25): for the write verbs `stashtab`, `bagtab`, `stashclose` and `playerwarp`, "Accept"; for `giveitem`, "Accept"; and for when `giveitem bag` ships, "Ship now, live 3 confirms (Recommended)". Live 3's V0 confirmed only the created unit's placement in map 0 and in the preferred grid, by `giveitem`'s own re-read in the same session, for a stackable material at count 1 (`confirmed: true`, key `0-0-212584560001-14`, `before=3 after=4`); V0 did not drag, save or reload the item, and the session's save backup was restored afterward. #14's own observation - that created units survived a drag, a save and a reload with no `ReportClient` call - is #14's, not live 3's. The same session's V0b tried a non-stackable template (class 18) and it was refused `give_refused` - the verb's reader `ApPreferredGrid` found no array `grid` in what `GetItemPreferredGrid(1, item)` returned, or the call failed - so only the stackable-material case is confirmed.
    - **What they do not prove.** `bagtab` proves `tabSelected`, not `activeNode` (the focus), which live 2 did not observe following a by-name call and live 3's V3 read as unchanged through the shipped verb; `stashtab` refuses a tab whose handler is not one live 2 reproduced (Unique's was never read); a `giveitem` count of 1 was measured for a stackable (V0); V0b's one class-18 non-stackable template was refused at the preferred-grid step (`ApPreferredGrid` found no `grid`), not at the count check, and no stack-size reader is measured.
    - **What they do not undo.** There is no by-name undo for a created item or a moved position, so a test that uses them restores its save backup afterwards - the hub's tools require one taken before the launch - and closes the stash (`stashclose`) before it stops, since the close is the stash's save (RUNTIME_DATA_MODELS § 17, the save invariant).
    - **Not built:** `stashopen` (live 2's one by-name open was followed by the game dying on the next command; causality not established; not re-called). Moving an item from the bag into the stash is ForgePact #68's `stashmoveall`/`stashmove` (item 39), not one of these verbs.
    - **Tests:** `tests/test_stash_bag_layout_contract.py` (`StashBagPlayerVerbs`, `MenuLayoutCellRows`); the hub's `tests/test_hs_drive_mcp_stash.py`.
31. **The relic filter now reads the five equipped relic slots, through the game's own fingerprint lookup by name (issue #93, 2.0.1, 2026-09-27):**
    - **What changed.** The scan is `hs-game-sdk`'s, for both bindings, so the fix is in the hub (`GetMaxedRelicIds`, `ScanEquippedRelicSlots`; `docs/RUNTIME_DATA_MODELS.md`). An item instance is a relic by its `itemType == 16`, the documented positive signal, with id and level read from its definition's `b` and `o`; before, the SDK looked for `c`, `cls` or `itemType` equal to 16, or a `relicLevel` field, on the structs it walked, which a game relic's definition does not match, and it never reached the equipped slots. The C++ scan reads slots 10-14 of `global.equippedItems[mplr][0]` and resolves each fingerprint string through `GetOnlinePlayerItemOwner` and then `GetItemFromFingerprint`, both by name, with the global instance as self and other. The Python binding reads a save's `equipped_items` entry by its key's class suffix.
    - **Measured (Live 1, research build):** the offline local character is player 1 (`mplr=1`), the equipped items live only in the global (the player instance has no `equippedItems`), and the arm-time line read `scan found 3 maxed relics (ids 109,124,135)` with `resolved=5 refused=0 relic=5 control=resolved stopped=none`, its five `slot:id@level` pairs equal to the save's.
    - **Not observed:** a relic drop held back live (relics roll only in Satanic zones; the arm-time line is the check instead of a relic farm); what the second array, `equippedItems[1][1]`, holds; any player index but 1 (co-op). The first of these could not have passed: the lever of that time wrote a field no relic pick reads, and 2.0.1 still dropped an equipped 10/10 relic (#125). Item 40 replaces the lever.
    - **Measured (Live 2, 2026-09-27, the pre-merge player DLL `c36004c6...8e6b`, which printed `v2.0.0`):** `on-relic-scan` passed - the relic filter logged `relicfilter: scan found 3 maxed relics (ids 109,124,135)` when it armed and again on a re-run, so the player build reads the equipped slots. The merged 2.0.1 DLL has not been run by any session.
    - **Tests:** the hub's `tests/test_relic_identification.py` and `tests/test_cpp_sdk.py` (`tests/cpp/test_sdk_player_hooks.cpp`); here `tests/test_relic_filter_contract.py` (`TestRelicFilterArmScanLine`) and `tests/test_relic_filter_behavior.py`.
32. **The Gold slider multiplies one coin's amount, so the gold a given setting gives has changed (issue #77, 2.0.1, 2026-09-27):**
    - **What changed, plainly.** At a Gold setting of `n`, gold from a monster is now `n` times the game's amount. Before, it was about `n²` times: the monster-gold script ran `n` times and each run's `DropGold` ran `n` times, so `n²` coins of the game's amount each. A player who ran x10 before got about 100 times the gold and now gets 10 times at the same setting; x100 went from 10,000 coins per drop, which froze the game (8.4 s at the drop, 6.5 s at the pickup, Live 1), to one coin. Gold from a `DropGold` call that does not come through `DropMonsterGold` was `n` coins and is now one coin of `n` times the amount - the same total, on the reading below. The owner chose this shape during Live 1: the multiplier scales the value of the coin, not the number of coins.
    - **What it rests on.** Argument 4 of `DropGold` as the coin's amount is Live 1's `goldtrace` reading - the one argument that varied per coin (51, 59, 31, 29) while the others stayed constant - plus the static reading that `DropGold` sets one coin's value from what it is handed. Live 2 (2026-09-27, the pre-merge player DLL `c36004c6...8e6b`; `on-gold-amount` pass) held it against the gold the game showed: at x100 the hook's line read `first coin 44 -> 4400`, and the HUD gold rose from 188948 to 189019 at x1 (+71) and on to 201889 at x100 (+12870, two stacks the screen showed as 5720 and 7150); the owner saw no freeze at the kill or at the pickup. So scaling argument 4 scales the gold picked up: the credited stacks were about 1.3 x the scaled argument 4 on this character (5720 = 4400 x 1.3), so what the pickup credits is argument 4 times a further factor whose source (a gold-find stat at pickup?) is not established. Monster gold only, one session, and not on the merged 2.0.1 DLL. The player build's `dropmult gold coin <i>/8` lines are what let a pickup's gold delta be held against that coin's own amount. A non-number or non-finite argument 4 is left alone, counted and named once (§ 2, `dropmult gold`).
    - **Not established:** whether the game caps or rounds a coin's value; the cause of a 284 ms frame at x1 in Live 1's baseline (no multiplier was active, so it is not #77's). `dropstats` still reports the monster-gold multiplier it was sent, though that original now runs once.
    - **Tests:** `tests/test_drop_gold_behavior.py` + `drop_gold_harness.cpp`.
33. **Mana Orb's countdown is an explicit object row, measured with the Chosen One upgrade only (issue #83, `manaorb-route: object-timer`, 2.0.1, 2026-09-27):**
    - **The route taken.** Live 1 found Mana Orb (talent 253) absent from the rule tier's rows, and with Chosen One the orb's own `destroyTimer` counting down across the cast (4151.71 to 1599.87 over about 18 s, `chosenOne` true, `orbitRadius` 0: the orb follows the player). So Mana Orb is the eighth `kSkillTimerRows` row, `manaOrb` on `White_Mage_Mana_Orb_obj`, with the 5040-frame start Live 1's duration sweep recorded as its full value; like every object row, the draw latches on the first positive reading it sees. No buff or talent-script route was built (out of scope; a follow-up).
    - **Not observed:** the orb without Chosen One (the cast was refused `cast_not_confirmed`, so `manaorb-timer-plain` has no reading), which is why the release notes claim only the Chosen One case; which script lengthens the timer from its Create's 432 frames to 5040; why the rule tier did not select talent 253.
    - **Measured (Live 2, 2026-09-27, the pre-merge player DLL `c36004c6...8e6b`):** `on-manaorb-countdown` passed - with Chosen One, the countdown arc showed on the Mana Orb slot right after the cast and was gone once the orb cleared. Not run on the merged 2.0.1 DLL.
    - **Tests:** `tests/test_toggle_skill_behavior.py` (`skilltimer/mana_orb_row_latches_and_draws/*`, with a never-latches-on-a-non-positive-reading negative control) and `tests/test_toggle_skill_contract.py` (the eight explicit rows); `docs/toggle-skills-research.md` § "Duration sweep (session 8)".
34. **Every refused craft press names its own reason, and a take whose `ItemCheckHash` did not run is never confirmed (issue #80, 2.0.1, 2026-09-27):**
    - **What changed.** The press gate's one `unreadable` became `already-served`, `unpaired`, `unnumbered-row` and `other-row`, each with its own counter and once-per-session line; the adapter's count edit now checks that its `ItemCheckHash` call dispatched, and when it did not the take is `hash-failed` and the press is refused (§ 2, `craftmats`). This rests on a reading of our own code, not a game measurement.
    - **Known imprecisions (review, 2026-09-27; neither changes a refusal or its line).** `hash-failed=` counts hash calls that did not dispatch, not presses: when a partial source edit's hash fails and the rollback's own count edit fails too, one refused press counts two and the loss is named after the hash failure, not the source mismatch that started the rollback. And the pending hash-failure flag is cleared only by a take report while the mod is on, so turning the mod off inside that one `CmTake` call and on again would name the next confirmed take a hash-failure loss.
    - **Measured (Live 2, 2026-09-27, the pre-merge player DLL `c36004c6...8e6b`):** one ordinary press moved a stack (`craftmats: moved 1 class=15 ...`, and the owner crafted one Old; `on-craftmats-press` pass), and none of the five refusal lines appeared anywhere in the session (`no-new-refusal` pass). The session's first press ran while the mods were not yet armed after `hs_launch` (why they did not arm on their own is not established), so it tested nothing; the re-press is the one that counts. Not run on the merged 2.0.1 DLL.
    - **Not observed live:** any of the five lines.
    - **Tests:** `tests/test_craft_mats_behavior.py` (the `target_press_refused_*` and `target_hash_failed_*` tests) and `tests/test_craft_mats_contract.py`.
35. **A loot item the filter hides is still a live instance, and what that costs a frame is not measured (issue #95 part 1, measurement only, 2026-09-27):**
    - **Measured (Live 1, `lootcensus`):** at the owner's strict filter after about 60 s of killing at `dropmult item 10`, `ground=291 hidden=281 invisible=281 coins=22`; with the filter off, `ground=291 hidden=0 invisible=95`. So the filter hides items without removing them, and the static reading says each keeps re-checking its visibility every 0.3 s of game speed. Coins are `Coin_obj` instances, not `Loot_Ground_obj`.
    - **Not observed:** the frame cost of hidden items (the strict window's `max 6535.4 ms` included #77's pickup stall, so no isolated reading exists - not evidence of a cost, and not evidence of none); what kept 95 items invisible with the filter off (the static reading has visibility follow the screen as well as the filter). The game has no "Show all loot" key; turning the filter off was the control.
    - **No mod ships.** #95 part 2 (dropping or removing filtered items) would first need an isolated frame-time window at a strict filter with nothing else in it. `docs/dev2-bug-batch-research.md` § "#95 part 1".
36. **The Pet Quest Collector moves on from a quest item it cannot collect, a change designed from a reading of our own code and confirmed by no session (2.0.1, 2026-09-27; no issue number):**
    - **Not #94.** This change was first filed under #94; the owner corrected the report on 2026-09-27 (#94 is the game's own companion loot pickup, item 37, `docs/pet-loot-stuck-research.md`). The change stays as an improvement of the collector.
    - **What changed.** The target is chosen by `PetQuestSelector` (`PetQuestCollectorMod.hpp`): the nearest collectable item on screen that is not held back. A travel that ends with the item still in place (no effect, gate, refused call, or a timeout whose collect-anyway did not remove it) holds that item back for 600 ticks, at most 32 items at a time, and counts it as `held back=` on `petquest 0`. The family walk keeps its 64-instance budget per tick but starts where the last one stopped, so an item past index 63 is reached within a few ticks.
    - **Why it is built this way.** Reading our own tick found no memory of failure and a walk that never passed its budget; no session named which of the four causes (no effect, gate, timeout, budget) the player met, so the fix answers all four at once and invents no collect. `docs/dev2-bug-batch-research.md` § "#94".
    - **Not observed live.** No Live 1 or Live 2 check measures this change, and the owner's own test that was to confirm it (the dev2 workorder's Live procedure 3, reading `petquest 0`'s lines on the player DLL) was not run, so the change is confirmed by no session, on the pre-merge player DLL or the merged 2.0.1 one; the 2.0.1 notes say it is not yet confirmed in a live game. Which counter rises besides `collected=` in a crowded spot is not established.
    - **Tests:** `tests/test_pet_quest_collector_behavior.py` + `pet_quest_collector_harness.cpp`, and the target-selection pins in `tests/test_pet_quest_collector_contract.py` (their comments still say #94, the collector's first filing).
37. **The pet moves on from loot it cannot pick up, built from a static reading; its one live session did not reproduce the stuck state (issue #94, `petunstick`, off by default, 2.0.1, 2026-09-27):**
    - **What changed.** A new Quality of Life switch, `mod_pet_loot_unstick` / `petunstick 1`. The game's own companion (`Companion_obj`) replaces its `lootTarget` only once that instance ceases to exist, and does not stop on arrival, so one ground item whose `PickupLoot` keeps failing pins the pet, hopping around it, for as long as it lies there. While on, `PetLootUnstickTick()` gives up a target the pet has stayed within 160 px of for 90 consecutive frames, and gives it up again every 90 frames while it stays the target and stays in reach (the watch re-arms at each give-up, so a target the game takes straight back is not kept): a `Loot_Ground_obj` gets `itemCompanionTimer` = 600 (the game's own "not for the pet yet" timer, about 10 s), the pet's `lootTarget` becomes -4 and its `lootList` is emptied (only when `ds_exists` confirms a live list), so the game's next scan picks something else. The timer is written only after `variable_instance_exists` says the item carries `itemCompanionTimer` (the name is a static reading, and a set on a missing name would create a stray variable and hold nothing); when it does not, nothing is written to the item, the target is still dropped and the list cleared, and the give-up is counted `timer absent=` with the last `object_index`, logged once. A coin has no such timer; it is only released, and counted again if it sticks again.
    - **What `petunstick 0` reports.** One line, `FullStatLine()` followed by `PetLootLocalStatSuffix()`: `held back=`, `coins released=`, `longest same-target=`, where the ticks went, the read-refusals (`unreadable=`, `list not cleared=`), then `give-up failed=`, `other kind=`, `timer absent=` and `re-picked while held=` with its `(ground <n> coin <n>)` split and the last ground timer read back. `held back=` counts the timer write returning, not the item staying out of the pet's next scan. So the tick remembers its last eight give-ups in the header's `PetLootRepickRing`, and a target the pet takes back within the 600-frame hold counts as `re-picked while held=` by kind, once per give-up: after a tick of no target or another target, or taken straight back on the very next tick (the game can rescan and take the same item in the Step right after the drop; which of its scan and its target pick runs first in one Step is not established). A target taken straight back is given up again 1.5 s later and counted again each time; a ground re-pick also reads the item's timer back. `longest same-target=` is the longest run between give-ups and cannot exceed 90 while the mod is on (below 90: nothing reached the count). Ground re-picks rising means the hold did not take; re-picks staying 0 while `held back=` rises means the pet is working through distinct items, one after another. Coin re-picks are expected. `timer absent=` above 0 has two causes a session cannot separate: the item lacks the name, or `variable_instance_exists` answers false for every item on this runner, which is not measured; record it, do not diagnose from it.
    - **Why it is built this way.** Everything it touches is an instance variable or list the game already reads, by name: no hook (the companion's events have no script-table entry), no address, no call into a game script, nothing collected, destroyed or credited. Detouring the event, calling `PickupLoot`/`instance_destroy`, dropping only the target, or changing `lootDistance` or the type filter were each rejected; `docs/pet-loot-stuck-research.md` § "The fix and what it does not do" says why. It does not make a failed pickup succeed: the item stays on the ground and the pet tries it again about ten seconds later.
    - **Not measured.** The mechanism is a static reading of the current build; which pickup failure a player meets (a full grid, a stack limit, a hash refusal), whether coins ever stick, and what the loot block's gate tests are not established.
    - **Live 1 (2026-09-28, the pre-merge player DLL `083d7e02…57c7`, which printed `v2.0.0`; slot 14):** the workorder `forgepact-pet-loot-stuck`'s one session. With the bag filled on the back end and the ground crowded (`dropmult item`, `runes`, `gems` and `ores` at 3), the owner played about 90 s with the mod off and about 90 s with it on and saw the pet stuck in neither phase. `off-pet-stuck` failed (the stuck state did not reproduce), `on-pet-moves-on` was not observed (`held back=`, `coins released=`, `timer absent=` and `other kind=` all stayed 0; at the end `ticks=27900`, `target gone=11691`, `longest same-target=13 frames`, under the 90-frame give-up, `re-picked while held=0`), and `unstick-counters` passed. So neither the stuck state nor the mod acting on it has been observed live: not observed, not evidence that either does not happen. The owner shipped the switch off by default on this result (2026-09-28), and the notes, the README and this item say it is not yet confirmed in a live game. The merged 2.0.1 DLL has not been run by any session. `docs/pet-loot-stuck-research.md` § "Live 1 results".
    - **Tests:** `tests/test_pet_loot_unstick_behavior.py` + `pet_loot_unstick_harness.cpp` (baseline and target scenarios against the real `PetLootUnstickMod.hpp`, including the pre-fix rules as the baseline `latched_watch_keeps_a_target_taken_straight_back` and the targets `ground_taken_straight_back_is_given_up_again`, `coin_taken_straight_back_is_given_up_again` and `repick_counted_once_per_give_up`; the re-pick decision is header code, so the harness runs it) and `tests/test_pet_loot_unstick_contract.py` (which also pins, on the comment-stripped source, that the timer write sits behind the `variable_instance_exists` answer, that `timer absent=` and `re-picked while held=` reach the `petunstick 0` line, and that every tick with a live target reaches the header's re-pick decision without returning on a target equal to the previous tick's, each with negative controls). No harness runs the ground item's timer branch (where a missing `itemCompanionTimer` counts `timer absent=`); it is pinned by shape only.
38. **RESOLVED 2026-09-28 — starting a panel sandbox cost about 4.5 s, 3.7 s of it `hs_game_sdk`'s package import; the SDK now loads its tables on first use:**
    - `import forgepact` imported `hs_game_sdk`, whose `__init__` imported every generated table, and building `hs_game_sdk.sprites.GameSprite`, an `IntEnum` of 32,271 members, took about 3.7 s on an idle machine (`py -3 -X importtime`), every time: the bytecode is cached, the enum is built at import. `forgepact.py` uses none of the sprites.
    - Every browser suite starts several sandboxes, so beside three other suites on four cores a start took up to 41 s. That is why `startSandbox` allows 120 s (see "Parallel load and the panel sandbox" under "The build half").
    - **The fix is in the hub's SDK.** `hs_game_sdk` now imports its five generated tables the first time one of their names is used, so the panel built only `objects` and `scripts` (none since ForgePact PR #116, below). On Python 3.13 (medians), `import forgepact` went from 4.44 s to 0.56 s and a sandbox start (spawn to `port=`) from 4.49 s to 0.76 s.
    - **The frozen build paid it too.** A ForgePact.exe built with `build_release.py`'s PyInstaller command answered `/api/state` 5.35 s after launch with the old SDK and 1.36 s with the new one. The CI-built 2.0.0 draft and 1.4.7 exes (Python 3.14) took 5.49 s and 6.38 s. No ForgePact change is needed: `forgepact-release.yml` checks `hs-game-sdk` out from the hub at build time, so the next package built after the hub change has it.
    - Numbers, the enum cost on each Python version, and why PyInstaller still bundles the tables: hs-game-sdk guide, "Import cost: the tables load on first use".
    - **The rest went in ForgePact PR #116 (2026-09-28).** `forgepact.py` also imported nine SDK names it never used (`GameObject`, `GameScript`, `StatId`, `PROC_FAMILIES`, `EquipmentSlot`, `PlayerEquipment`, `scan_relic_levels`, `ModDefinition`, `GLOBAL_MOD_REGISTRY`), so every panel start still built `objects` and `scripts`. It now imports only `SATANIC_BUFFS` and `SATANIC_DEBUFFS`, keeping the `sys.path` fallback and the `except Exception` fallback to empty pools, and the panel builds no table. The published 2.0.0 exe was built at hub `404ad79` (release run 36405528802), which has the lazy SDK, so 2.0.0 builds only those two tables at a start; the first package with this PR builds none.
      - **Measured.** Medians of fresh interpreters, ForgePact `00d1a26` against the branch, both in the hub layout on hub `4dba20f`'s SDK, interleaved on a 20-core machine carrying other sessions' load. Python 3.13, 15 runs: `import forgepact` 0.555 s → 0.210 s, a sandbox start (spawn to `port=`) 0.764 s → 0.455 s. Python 3.10, 7 runs: 2.356 s → 0.181 s and 2.499 s → 0.316 s. `py -3 -X importtime`, 7 runs: `forgepact` cumulative 0.569 s → 0.211 s. Neither side has an `hs_game_sdk.objects` or `.scripts` line, because the SDK imports a table through `importlib.import_module`, which `-X importtime` does not log; the tables' cost shows as `forgepact`'s own time instead, 0.353 s → 0.003 s.
      - **The exe.** Built with `build_release.py`'s PyInstaller command (3.13, PyInstaller 6.20), the base exe answered `/api/state` 1.31 s after launch and the branch's 0.99 s (7 interleaved runs, throwaway `USERPROFILE`). Neither warn file names an `hs_game_sdk` module, so the packaging guard passes; `tools/panel_smoke.py` passed three runs, and every run of every build served 25 Satanic buffs and 26 debuffs. The PYZ still holds all twelve SDK entries, because the SDK's `TYPE_CHECKING` block names every table; the panel just never loads one (Packaging Hazards item 4).
      - **A test assumed a slow start.** `tests/test_start_sandbox_report.py` gave a seeded sandbox 0.5 s to report its port and expected it to miss. Branch starts took 0.35-0.55 s, so in 2 of 5 runs the sandbox started in time: the case failed, and the sandbox it had started kept node running until the 600 s timeout. ForgePact #128 made the case deterministic (the late sandbox waits `--start-delay` 10 s, and every sandbox that starts is stopped; see the `tests/` list), so #116 leaves the test as #128 wrote it. On #116's branch #128's test passed 4 of 4 runs.
      - **Tests.** ForgePact's `tests/test_panel_sdk_import.py` (Repository Layout, `tests/`) and the hub's `tests/test_sdk_lazy_import.py`, whose `FORGEPACT_NAMES` pins the same two names.
39. **Move all into the stash (`stashmoveall`, F4; ForgePact #68, 2.1.0) moves items through the game's own routines at a moment the game did not choose, and re-reads only the stash tab on show - an accepted risk (2026-09-28):**
    - **What it does.** With the panel's switch on and the stash open, the in-game **Move all** button left of the backpack's Sort button, F4 or `stashmoveall run` moves each item of the bag view on show into the stash tab on show, one at a time, by the routines the game's own Ctrl + left click runs, called by name in the order and with the arguments the research replayed (`ForgePact/docs/stash-move-research.md` § Decision, § Ship design). What the tab has no room for, or does not take, stays in the bag; a loss stops the run and turns the mod off for the session.
    - **The accepted risk.** The owner's request (2026-09-27): "Call the game's own per-item move, resolved by name, once per item. Never rewrite the item containers or map entries directly, and never use a hand-resolved address. Change nothing else the game does." On overflow (2026-09-28): "it should only stash those items that displayed stash tab have space for." And on the stash tabs no container can be read for, asked whether no-spill may rest on the route plus a re-read of the shown tab only, with the save comparison as the check and this item saying so: "Accept (Recommended)".
    - **The other stash tabs are not re-read in game.** The ordinary tabs' `[tab][x][y]` array has no name found (RUNTIME_DATA_MODELS § 17), `Controller_obj` has no personal-page grid, and `GridAddItem`'s answer carries `tabNumber=0` on shared tab 1 as well, so nothing can say by name which tab an item landed on. After each call the adapter re-reads `stashTabSelected` (a change is a loss) and the shown tab's own array only. No-spill therefore rests on the route - each routine is handed only the shown tab's array, and only after that array read room for the item - and on the research: the game's own quick move against a full tab and the by-name placement against it both left the item in the bag and tried no other tab. The check in play is Live 2's `case-full`, which compares every stash container's saved keys before and after.
    - **The in-game button, and why it has no activation** (the decision, for every ForgePact UI button: [ADR 0005](../../adr/0005-forgepact-ui-buttons-poll-for-a-press.md)). The node is a `UI_Button_Small_obj` named `ForgePactMoveAll` (its `uiNodeCallstack`), made by the game's own `UiCreateNode` and taken away by `UiRemoveNode`, both by name with self the `UI_Stash_obj` window, placed from the Sort node found by its call-stack name `InventorySort`. Its activation is left undefined and nothing is hooked for it: the press is read by the frame poll (`mouse_check_button_pressed`, the mouse's GUI point, the node's bbox read at that frame), which duplicates the game's own hit test. The activation route was dropped after Live 1f: a click on a node bound to `UiSetFloatingToFalse` reached the plugin through the node's own click event (user event 15, self the node, other the window, one argument, the node's `activationArgs`) and then ended the game with "bool argument is unset" inside that script, which is written for another object. Live 1g measured the unbound node: on a click on it, no armed routine logged a call with the node as self and no dialog appeared (that nothing runs is the static reading of an undefined activation), and the poll counted it once, a click beside it counted only as a press elsewhere, a tab switch kept it, `UiRemoveNode` removed it, and the stash's close destroyed it. Not read: how the game creates the Sort button, and the stash window's step body that decides which node gets a click. Fail-safe: a node that cannot be made is reported once (`stashmoveall: button - <reason>; F4 still works`), a stash open with no visible `InventorySort` node is said once a session (`stashmoveall: button - not shown: ...`, so a game patch renaming it is not silence), and neither turns the mod off; it is shown only while the switch is on and the stash and a visible Sort are listed, and removed on switching off and on a loss. A click that moved nothing is not silence: the state line (bare `stashmoveall`) carries the session's press counts (`button=`, `presses=`, `in_node=`, `outside=`, `unread=`, `errors=`, `taken=`, `dropped=`, `last_drop=`), which name a poll that saw no press, a press that missed the box or read no box, a poll that threw, or a press the F4 guard dropped and why; Live procedure 2's `button-press` reads it after the click. Switched off while `UiRemoveNode` leaves the node listed, the retry runs at the ensure step's pace (every tenth frame), not every frame.
    - **The Socketable tab, by name since Live 1f.** A socketable whose identity has a node on the tab merges by the shape Live 1f and Live 1g measured (`socketMergeRoute`), fed from the bag's Socket view only; the tab is read as its `StashSocketGrid` nodes, since `Controller_obj.stashSocketItemSlot` is not the container. A merge was measured with a count of 1 only (an orb and a gem), and `wholeStackMerge` was measured on the Materials tab only, so the Socketable tab reads its own flag, `socketWholeStackMerge`, off: a socketable stack of more than one stays in the bag (`a socketable merge of more than one unit is not measured`), while a single one still merges. A new kind has no measured by-name placement (no accepted kind absent from the tab could be obtained without a person), so it stays in the bag (`a new kind stays in the bag`). The game refuses jewels and Gems of Incarnation there before any placement; those stay too.
    - **No measured by-name shape, and what the mod does instead.** A stash page from a bag sub-tab: refused (`unsupported bag tab -4 for stash tab <n>`). A merge on a stash page: done with the Materials tab's measured merge shape and the page's own two numbers, but measured on the Materials tab only. A stackable on a shared page: its stack there cannot be identified by name (a shared page's entries answer on no map), so it is skipped (`its stack on the shown tab could not be read`). The Unique tab and the bag's Key, Tarot and Relic views: refused.
    - **Not observed** in play before the acceptance session: every case of the shipped adapter (the research replayed each routine one call at a time through `craftprobe`, and made and polled the button through the research build's probe, not this adapter); F4 itself; a click on the shipped button; whether the Sort button stays visible on the bag's Materials and Socket views (the button is shown only while it is); a socketable merge of more than one unit; a run of many items in one frame; a multi-cell item into the Materials tab; the undo (a placed item whose bag cell did not clear is taken back out of the shown tab by `GridRemoveItem`, the research's undo shape, and the owner step reversed if it ran); and whether the Materials array's answer cell is `[x][y]` or `[y][x]` (both orders are accepted as confirming the placement).
    - **Live confirmation (2026-09-28, Live procedure 2).** The player build, `BloodPactPlugin.dll` SHA-256 `64a55e8d66c1f89c5c766c8f1b1b8e0a0f00869e42751d45121a9bd4ff8b16ed`, on save slot 14, fully automatic, no moved key returned to the bag by hand, the saves restored afterwards; all 19 checks passed (`ForgePact/docs/stash-move-research.md` § Live 2 results) and the game never ended. It was the procedure's third run: the first (the same plugin source, SHA-256 `4171ad74...b5e1e5a2`) wrote its checks where the check reader could not read them, and the second found no tab the procedure then counted as nearly full and had keys moved back by hand, so the procedure was amended and run again. **Observed:** with the switch off, F4, `stashmoveall run` and the button each did nothing (the cells unchanged with no log line, `refused - off`, no button listed), and F4 with the switch on started five runs in the same session, the positive control for that zero; `stashmove` moved one item; the **button** was listed beside Sort as `ForgePactMoveAll`, a scripted click on it started one run (`presses=1 in_node=1 taken=1`) that moved 6 of 6 items into the Personal tab, and switching off removed it (`off-baseline-button`, `button-press`: the button shipped, on the frame-poll route); Shared tab 1 took all 4 items handed to it and Shared tab 2 took none of the next 4 (`no room on the shown tab`), with Shared tab 3 and the Personal tab unchanged cell for cell across that press (each read against its state just before it; the refill had taken 4 items from Shared tab 3) and, in the saved `stash.hss`, only the three containers the session moved into or took from changed; the Materials tab refused 4 non-stackables, gave three new material kinds a cell each, and took a second item of one kind out of the bag with the mod reporting `-> stack` into the stack the same run had just made; the saved files held each of the 14 moved keys once and the merged key nowhere. That stack's count was not read after the run, so these readings do not tell the merge apart from a loss. **Not observed:** the held-modifier guard, the Socketable tab's merge through the shipped adapter (all 19 bag socketables were kinds the tab lacked, so each stayed as `a new kind stays in the bag`; the merge is measured on the research build only, Live 1f and 1g), a merge on a stash page, a stackable on a Shared page, the undo, an owner step that did not take, any loss, the fail-safe lines, whether Sort stays visible on the bag's Materials and Socket views, and a multi-cell item into the Materials tab. **Seen and not fixed:** in the first run, switched on again while the stash stays open, the button did not come back until a stash tab was clicked (then a new node was listed); the cause is not established, the third run clicked a tab rather than test it again, and F4 and the verb are not affected. The bag for the full-tab case was refilled by the game's own Ctrl + left click, since `hs_give_item` refuses grid-tab items (`docs/tools/hs-drive-mcp.md`, Known limitations).
    - **The round-2 review's findings, closed.** The route is decided again at the point of use from the stack sum re-read on the shown tab before each item's call (`RouteAtUse`), so a second bag item of one identity merges into the stack the first made instead of duplicating a unit; any held Alt, Ctrl or Shift is no key edge, and the button's press takes the same guard, so Alt+F4 starts nothing; the owner step's dispatch and the key's map 0 answer after it, and the second `ValidateItem`'s answer, enter the report, an owner step that did not take being a loss; after a loss the state line reads `state=off-for-this-session <the button's counts> reason=<reason>` (the counts kept, so a click that ended in a loss reads from the same line), `stashmoveall 1` answers why it stays off, and the panel shows `off (this session)`; and the shared reference and the ship design say every merge was measured on the Materials tab.
    - **Tests:** `tests/test_stash_move_all_behavior.py` + `tests/stash_move_all_harness.cpp` (the core: off by default, the plan, the routes Live 1e, 1f and 1g decided, the room check, the shown-tab rule, the loss rule, the button's node, press and fail-safe, the lines); `tests/test_stash_move_all_contract.py` (the adapter, the button block, the Socketable tab's read, the frame gate, the panel row, README and notes).

40. **Remove owned relics answers `GetRelicQuest`, the relic pick's own draw-again check, and reads the relic tab (ForgePact #125, hub #324, 2.1.0, 2026-09-30):**
    - **What was wrong.** 2.0.1 wrote each maxed relic's `droprate.base` to 1e18 around `DropRelic` and restored it after; its `holding back N of M` line proved only that the write landed. A static reading of the Sep-17 build (hub `docs/models/relic-pick-spec.md`) shows no relic pick reads that field: `DropRelic` draws `irandom(155)` and draws again while `GetRelicQuest` answers true (true for the quest relics 141..155 only), then may copy an equipped relic below level 10 in place of the pick. `EnemyKillSatanicZoneRelic` and `...Feast` run the same draw and never pass through `DropRelic`. The scan also missed the relic tab. So the reported relic (140, equipped at 10/10) dropped at its vanilla rate, 1 in 141 relic drops.
    - **What it does now.** `FrameCallback` installs `Hook_GetRelicQuest` once a player exists, and only a native detour counts: YYC calls the script directly, and a direct-call scan of the build found exactly three callers, the three relic routines. The hook asks the game first. For a relic the player owns at 10/10 it answers true while another relic is left (`RelicFilterMod::QuestAnswer`, `AnyRelicLeft`, the game's own answer per id probed once), so the game's loop draws again and the other relics keep equal odds; with every droppable relic maxed it stands down, since the loop would otherwise never end. The maxed set is scanned at most once per frame (`MaxedForFrame`) and includes the relic tab through the SDK's `ScanRelicTab` (`Controller_obj.inventoryData[key].inventoryRelicGrid`, key 1 online or the player row offline, the rule the game's own `PickupRelic` uses). AFK FARM's reward scope gets the game's answer untouched. `Hook_DropRelic` is only `dropmult relic`'s now, and no filter code writes the drop table.
    - **Tests:** hub `tests/test_relic_pick_model.py` (baseline, target, stand-down, and lever parity with this module's source), `tests/test_cpp_sdk.py` (`TestRelicTab`), `tests/test_relic_identification.py` (`TestSaveRelicTab`); here `tests/test_relic_filter_behavior.py` and `tests/test_relic_filter_contract.py` (`TestRelicFilterLever`).
    - **Live 1 (2026-09-30, dev build `D7C1815E...`, character Suh; hub `hs-game-sdk/curated/relic_pick_measurements.json`):**
      - **Setup.** Suh wore 68, 140, 15 and 29 at 10/10 and 109 at 2; the relic tab held 92 and 128 at 10/10.
      - **Hook.** `relicfilter: hook installed -> ON (GetRelicQuest, native detour)`.
      - **Arm scan.** `scan found 6 maxed relics (ids 15,29,68,92,128,140)`. The relic tab line read `nodes=100 ... relic=100 ... maxed=92@10,128@10 stopped=none`, equal to the save.
      - **Filter off.** 8 of 150 built relics were maxed.
      - **Filter on.** 0 of 150 were maxed, with 10 skips logged.
      - **Every droppable id but 7 and 109 test-maxed.** Only 7 and 109 came out, ten each.
      - **Quest relics.** No quest relic came out in 320 drops.
      - **The tab route's shape was measured in this session.** Two dev builds failed before it: the route first stopped at `inventoryData`, then read no fingerprints. So the SDK reads index `key - 1` and a node's `nodeFingerprint`.
      - **Restored.** ForgePact 2.0.0's DLL and the saves were restored or verified afterwards.
      - Capture: workorder `forgepact-125-relic-reroll-live-1.md`, 6/6 checks passed.

41. **Skill Haste and All Skills in `statadd` (ForgePact #114, hub #337, 2.1.0, 2026-09-30):**
    - **What it is.** Stat Forge's last two boosts, ported: `statadd` is now a three-entry additive table in `StatsManager.hpp` (`castrate`, `skillhaste`, `allskills`), each adding its bonus to element 0 of its `Stat*` script's array on a copy, after the game's calculation. The panel shows them in Modifiers → Offense (`PERCENT_STATS` rows: Skill Haste 0-200 step 5, All Skills 0-100 step 1, both off by default; All Skills reads `+N`, not `+N%`).
    - **The game side** (hub `docs/models/skill-stat-spec.md`, `RUNTIME_DATA_MODELS` § 7.5): `ReturnSpecificStat` dispatches stat 103 to `StatSpellHaste` and stat 2 to `StatAllSkills`, their only caller. `Controller_obj`'s Step lowers a skill cooldown's time left by `(1 + haste × 0.005) × deltaSpd` each step, so a cooldown lasts base / (1 + haste/200).
    - **Why the Skill Haste slider stops at 200, not Stat Forge's 500.** Live 1 measured that the game counts at most 200: totals of 240 and 340 ended Blade Barrier's cooldown in exactly as many steps as 200 did.
    - **Why only the native route arms.** YYC calls both scripts directly, so a table-only hook would log `HOOK INSTALLED` and add nothing; the install is refused with a line naming the route instead (the same rule as the relic filter's `GetRelicQuest`).
    - **The first-call line** (both builds) is how a player build shows the hook fired, since `BP_DIAG_INCREMENT` compiles away under `FORGEPACT_RELEASE`: `statadd StatSpellHaste: first boosted call 40 -> 140`.
    - **Tests:** hub `tests/test_skill_stat_model.py` (baseline, target, the cap, lever parity with this module's source, and `MeasuredTests` against `hs-game-sdk/curated/skill_stat_measurements.json`); here `tests/test_stat_add_behavior.py` + `tests/stat_add_harness.cpp` and `tests/test_stat_add_contract.py`, and the derived behaviour oracle's `NATIVE_SLIDERS`.
    - **Live 1 (2026-09-30, dev build `2311E933...`, character Suh, a Samurai with 40 Skill Haste from gear, in town):**
      - **Hooks.** `HOOK INSTALLED on StatSpellHaste` and `on StatAllSkills`, no TABLE-ONLY line; `statadd list` read `hook=native` for both.
      - **Method.** Blade Barrier (137, 8 s cooldown) cast from code with `tgprobe cast 137`; its cooldown counted as the Skill Haste reads the hook saw while it ran (the hook installed as a pass-through first, so the no-bonus runs count). One read per step, 60 a second, and none while no cooldown ran.
      - **Skill Haste.** First-call lines `40 -> 140` and `40 -> 340`. Steps: 392 and 397 with no bonus, 280 (+100), 265 (+120), 238 (+160, total 200), 238 (+200), 238 (+300).
      - **Interrupted.** At 23:57 another tool closed the game through AFK FARM's `game_session.py close` (a clean exit) and relaunched it for its own work, so the All Skills and save checks ran in a second session (below).
      - **Found on the way.** `ReturnTalentLevel` has no direct caller in this build, and calling it by name with only an id raises "I32 argument is undefined" (`skillprobe state`'s level lines); the game carried on.
    - **Live 1, session 2 (2026-10-01, dev build `42FD7566...`, the same character):**
      - **All Skills.** For Honor (142, one point) cast from code at 0 and at +19. First-call line `statadd StatAllSkills: first boosted call 28 -> 47`. Its buff (type 42) read `buffValue=[137.8,72.5,0]` at 0 and `[228,120,0]` at +19 (`skillprobe state bind=id:<buff>.buffValue`): the level times [4.75, 2.5], at 29 (1 point + 28 from gear) and 48.
      - **Blade Barrier** carries no level-dependent variable on its object: only its timers and orbit changed between casts at 0, +19 and 0.
      - **`ReportClient`:** 0 calls through both sessions, on `skillprobe`'s row with its controls climbing.
      - **Save.** After `game_session.py close` (clean exit), Suh's save differed from the copy taken before the session only in `playtime`, and `shop.ini` only in `class_time8`; talents, sub-talents, inventory and quests were equal once their JSON and quest order were normalised.
      - **Restored.** ForgePact 2.0.0's DLL (7AD9AF2457AA...) is back and verified.
      - Capture: workorder `forgepact-114-skill-haste-all-skills-live-1.md`.

---

## Maintenance Triggers

- **A new test module:** it must pass alone as well as in the serial run, because `tools/run_tests_parallel.py` runs each module in its own process alongside others. Import what it needs itself (put `src/` on `sys.path` before loading `src/forgepact.py`), bind port 0, and write only under a directory unique to it (`tempfile`, or its own `build/<name>`). Check with `py -3 -m unittest discover -s tests -p <module>.py` from `ForgePact/`. A module that starts a browser declares `PARALLEL_GROUP`; one that measures timing declares `PARALLEL_EXCLUSIVE = True`, so it runs alone at the end. A new panel browser suite gets a module of its own (not a method in an existing one, since the runner parallelises by module), imports `_missing` and `_npm` from `tests/panel_browser.py`, and declares `PARALLEL_GROUP = "panel-browser"`; `tests/test_run_tests_parallel.py` fails if it does not.
- **Game Executable Updates:** When a new Season 10 patch releases, verify that spawner object names, GML function names, and `LoadDrops` drop family indices remain valid. The player build contains **no raw game addresses** to re-verify (Known Limitations item 11) — everything resolves by name or off a YYToolkit struct — so a new build should surface as named lookups failing, not as a crash. Player-build closure names (`anon@N@...`) come from `HeroSiege::Scripts` constants, so regenerating hs-game-sdk after a patch that moves one surfaces it as a compile error and a failing `test_player_build_closure_names_all_match_the_sdk`, instead of a silent runtime name-lookup failure. The dev-only `kCiCallMethodFnRva` does need re-verifying before anyone runs `citrace collect confirm native`; `citrace dispatchdump` / `citrace symdump` in the research build are the tools for re-locating it.
  - **Closure names move with every game patch.** The player build now spells them through `HeroSiege::Scripts` constants (above), so a regeneration surfaces as a compile error rather than a silent hook. Done (2026-09-18): player names now match hub `4539e68`'s SDK, regenerated for the current game build (`data.win` `07D864C9…`) - e.g. `GenerateItemHash@anon@4645` is `GenerateItemHash@anon@4791` there - on ForgePact branch `fix/closure-names-current-game`, merged into `feat/prospect-window-research`. The 25 research-only `citrace` closure literals moved with them; the seven `Quest_Object_Parent_obj` and ten `Profile_Manager_obj` closures were mapped by ordinal position within their Create event (equal counts, monotone offset growth), so the `m_Quest*` method-name labels the research code attaches carry over by inference only, not observed on the current build. `test_citrace_closure_hooks_cover_every_sdk_closure_of_their_objects` now fails the next regeneration loudly (naming which closures exist and which are hooked) instead of drifting silently. ForgePact's release CI (`forgepact-release.yml`) builds against hub `main`'s SDK, so hub #74 (the SDK regeneration) and this fix must land together - merging one without the other breaks the release build one way or the other. `prospectprobe`'s table already uses SDK constants.
- **YYToolkit Header / Binary Sync:** Any rebuild of `YYToolkit.dll` requires recompiling `BloodPactPlugin` against matching headers in `plugin_build\include\` to prevent vtable mismatch crashes. Because the plugin now reads `CScriptRef` directly (under `/DYYTK_DEFINE_INTERNAL=1`), a header update also has to keep those layouts truthful — the headers' own `static_assert(sizeof(YYObjectBase) == 0x88)` is the compile-time check, and `petquest stat`'s `REFUSED (structural)` line is the runtime one.
  - The hub's `third_party/yytoolkit/` series is pinned to the same upstream commit as these headers (`5a95e46`, tag v4.0.1) and touches none of the plugin-facing files (`YYToolkit/source/YYTK/Shared/`, `ExamplePlugin/`), so a DLL built from it is meant to keep working with a plugin compiled against the unmodified pinned headers. One behaviour does change for a plugin: `CreateCallback(EVENT_OBJECT_CALL)` returns `AURIE_UNAVAILABLE` instead of succeeding with a callback that never fires. `BloodPactPlugin` registers `EVENT_FRAME` only, so this is not expected to affect it — observed live on 2026-09-19: `BloodPactPlugin` v1.4.4 loaded and initialized against a build of this series, `EVENT_FRAME` kept firing (it drives the plugin's IPC poll), and zero `REFUSED EVENT_OBJECT_CALL` lines were logged in that idle session (see `third_party/yytoolkit/README.md`, "Second launch results (2026-09-19, plugin loaded)").
- **Dependency Upgrades (YYToolkit):** A change to the distributed YYToolkit — a new upstream version, a fix, a build flag — is made in the hub, not here: move the pin in `third_party/yytoolkit/upstream.json`, refresh or add patches under `third_party/yytoolkit/patches/` (each with its mandatory header), and rebuild with `tools/build_yytoolkit.py`; that directory's README has the procedure and the launch gate. What has to survive an upstream bump is the whole of `patches/series`, not "the disk cache and the `ExecuteIt` change" — that two-item list described the old notice, and the old notice did not describe the binary. Never rebuild `YYToolkit.dll` from `yytoolkit-modified/` or from any tree that is not the pinned commit plus the series.
- **Moving the `YYToolkit.dll` pin (merged, PR 53; not yet shipped in a release):** PR 53 (1) repointed `tools/toolchain-pins.json` at the hub release asset for a launch-gated build of the hub series, with its SHA-256 and the hub commit as provenance — the pin count stays eleven; (2) rewrote `yytoolkit-modified/NOTICE.md` to point at the hub directory and deleted the two whole-file copies in the same commit; (3) corrected `CREDITS.md` and `README.md`, which used to say "one modified source file"; (4) ships the notice and `YYToolkit-BUILD-INFO.json` with the binary — `build_release.py` now copies both into `modfiles/` alongside `README.md`, `CREDITS.md` and `LICENSE`; (5) carries `release-notes-v1.4.4.md`, naming what was and was not launch-tested rather than claiming a settled fix; (6) deleted `ensure_ri_cache`'s hand-measured RVA table from `src/forgepact.py`, which patch `0001` made unnecessary. It merged to `origin` after the launch gate carried a plugin-loaded row and the owner's confirmation, together with HS-Offline-Tracker's equivalent (PR 3) — see `third_party/yytoolkit/README.md`, "Where the binary is published". The hub release its pin names exists and is published (not marked `--latest`), and the asset URL it points at resolves. **What remains is the owner's decision to tag and release ForgePact 1.4.4** (and HS-Offline-Tracker 0.1.3 alongside it, since both install to `mods/aurie/YYToolkit.dll` with overwrite semantics) — that is a separate, still-outstanding step from either the merge or the published library release.
- **A control added to, or changed on, the panel:** the markup goes in the tab it belongs to, `panel/src/tabs/*.svelte` (shared logic in the plain-JS modules under `panel/src/`), and never back into `src/forgepact.py`, which only decides what the control's `/api/set` sends. Never re-record the behaviour oracle: `panel/tests/behaviour-oracle.json` is the legacy page's recording and the only proof the port kept every old control's behaviour (its `oracle:record` npm script passes `--legacy`; this checkout no longer embeds the pre-port page, and an older ref's page is served only through the sandbox server's `--src`, which records a supplement, never the legacy file). Instead add the new or changed control to the derivation in `panel/tests/oracle-derive.mjs`, re-run `npm run oracle:derive` in `panel/`, and commit the regenerated `panel/tests/behaviour-oracle-derived.json` with the change, never hand-editing any oracle JSON file - Representative Change Workflow step 3 says the same, and `npm run oracle:replay` fails its coverage check on an enumerated control that no oracle file lists. A control a pre-port legacy page had but the legacy recording predates (the Gems of Incarnation controls, from origin/main at `f1e2f57`) is covered by the recorded supplement `panel/tests/behaviour-oracle-gems.json` (`npm run oracle:record:gems -- --src <dir> --source-rev <sha>`) and by `npm run e2e:gems`; a change to those controls is re-checked against both. When a supplement-recorded control moves to another tab or card, the move goes into `SUPPLEMENT_RELOCATION` in `panel/tests/lib/oracle-relocate.mjs` (navigation steps only; it refuses a step that sent anything), then `npm run oracle:derive`, never into the recording and never by re-recording it from this tree. A key a later origin/main adds to `KEYS` is covered by a supplement recorded from main's page (as `panel/tests/behaviour-oracle-primeevil.json` is, through `record --only primeevil` and replay's `--key-supplement`) plus an entry in `ADDED_KEYS` for `insertAddedKeys` in `panel/tests/lib/oracle-relocate.mjs`, the reset line the backend now adds to every full key reset the older recordings hold, never by editing a recording; `insertAddedKeys` throws on a recording that already sends the line. Rebuild with `npm --prefix ForgePact/panel run build` before replaying or packaging, and write its contract test's page pins through `tests/panel_source.py` (`panel_source()` or `panel_file('<file>')`), not by reading a built file or `forgepact.py`. A visual change also needs a fresh screenshot baseline (`run screens`), compared deliberately rather than accepted. After any `panel/src` change, rebuild and run `npm --prefix ForgePact/panel run e2e:perf`: it must stay `26/26`, and a check that fails on a noisy machine gets more `--runs`, never a looser budget. After a motion change (anything in `app.css` that transitions, `@starting-style`, `[data-instant]`, or the JS that sets it), also run `npm --prefix ForgePact/panel run e2e:motion` and add a `motion-M<k>` check for a new motion (and after a change to the theme picker, the check marks, the gutters or anything else the finish review fixed, `npm --prefix ForgePact/panel run e2e:finish`, then update `panel/DESIGN.md`): motion uses the `--motion-*` tokens only, never runs on anything a poll repaints or on a keyboard action, and under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` removes movement and scale while opacity and colour fades stay, as the export's `motion.notes` says (`amendments.ship`).
- **A Figma design export made or re-made (`panel/design/figma-export.json`):** give each `components[]` entry the `selector` the panel draws it as, and each `selectorTokens` entry that belongs to one its `component`, and make `node ForgePact/panel/tests/design-match.mjs --structure --export <export> --out <dir>` exit 0 an acceptance criterion of the export workorder itself, not of the restyle after it. In the UI redesign (2026-09-24..27) the export disagreed with the panel's page seven times (value strings, 68 texts, a bare `.val` matching outside its stepper, a font path, a baked-in version), each found only at restyle, at the cost of two restyle rounds, two Figma rounds and three replans (hub `docs/agents/workorder-calibration.md`, "Why the ForgePact UI redesign took 60 hours (2026-09-27)", added by hub PR #264 and measured from the redesign's transcripts and context Logs; the earlier "about 48 hours" in "Spending each check once" was read from plan-file dates). The committed export predates `component`, so its structure run reports `placement: no selectorTokens entry names a component` until the next export adds them.
